# App-wide review — 23 October 2026

Status: in progress. This report does not claim release completion.

The owner requested a second review of every OwnDashboard page, fixes for verified issues, lower operating cost, updated documentation, meaningful commits and a merge into main. Work continues in the isolated audit checkout; the owner's unrelated checkout remains intact. The baseline is `dba5fc8`, including the completed DNESKAi audit implementation. Historical reports retain their original evidence dates.

## Review order and coverage

Each destination needs source/data-flow review, relevant business and failure checks, and rendered verification. An inventory entry alone is not a review. `source-inventory.json` records the baseline source files and their review status.

| Area | Destinations | State |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared boundaries | Authenticated catch-all, shell, navigation, query cache, initial loading | Reviewing |
| Execution | Home, Work, Inbox, Tasks, Calendar, Goals, Dates | Home/Tasks/Calendar and captured weekly saves checked; aggregate/monitoring boundaries and final release pending |
| Projects and clients | Projects list and all workspace tabs, Competition, Clients, Opportunities | Project/client/Competition targeted checks pass; opportunity/shared metadata and final release pending |
| Career | Board, saved roles, applications, history, letters | Captured reads/writes and recovery checked; workspace/list density, remaining providers/monitoring and final matrix pending |
| Finance | Money, Accounts, Transactions, Categories, Subscriptions, Invoices and print | Core finance/bank/import/rule and invoice save/QR checks pass; legacy documents/remaining writes and final release pending |
| Knowledge | Notes, Prompts, Tools, Links, IG TIPS, References | All six Knowledge destinations have targeted review/checks; shared integration and final release checks remain |
| Marketing | Review, calendar, knowledge, results, Workflows, DNESKAi workspace | Daily mix/source admission and small guard helpers checked; workspace/provider repairs and final matrix pending |
| Account and public boundaries | Settings, Login, Guest, unknown routes, dev-preview guard, OAuth, PWA | Login/PWA/OAuth/private-preference checks pass; remaining integration/session boundaries and final release pending |
| Backend and operations | API routes, cron/provider budgets, database ownership and query plans | Reviewing |
| Release | Current-doc reconciliation, full validation, additive migrations, PR, main merge, deployment | Pending |

The [working visual plan](visual-plan.html) highlights compact Task rows and retained drafts and annotates authentic preview screenshots of the changed login, repository notes, project operations, repository activity, notifications/mobile navigation, Money, cron-history, project-edit recovery, guest-notice, source-edit, daily-mix and invoice-save recovery components. It has desktop/mobile switches and keeps remaining areas explicitly pending. Rebuild it with `node scripts/build-app-audit-visual-plan.mjs` after updating this report or the inventory.

Source-inspection follow-ups are tracked in [remaining-review.md](remaining-review.md). They are leads and unfinished work, not passing findings.

## Findings under review

1. Fixed: failed server reads now render a bilingual retry state before mounting the shell. A successful missing-project read still returns 404; an outage does not. In-shell navigation also waits for all required entity stores before mounting writable panels. Verified cached records remain available during background failures.
2. Fixed: both on-demand destinations reject partial read failures. Opportunities surfaces the failure and no longer records a failed platform-directory request as a successful zero-platform check.
3. Fixed: sidebar projection fallback now checks whether projects were actually loaded, so a loaded empty list stays empty. Destinations already loading projects reuse that read for navigation rather than making a duplicate query.
4. Fixed: 24 destination panels now load through top-level dynamic imports, preserving SSR and the persistent shell. On the same optimized fixture build and browser, initial Home script transfer fell from 1,038,179 to 625,902 bytes (39.7%); decoded script bytes fell from 3,560,086 to 1,996,846 (43.9%). The script count changed from 26 to 27 because chunks are smaller. Raw resource measurements are committed beside this report. All 25 visible destinations passed the six-width reflow/title matrix (150 rendered states); full interaction checks remain pending.
5. Fixed: Marketing no longer loads three unused shell entity families; Notes loads only notes and projects. Component dependencies and route-contract tests confirm the smaller boundary.
6. Fixed: the public tour previously relied on anonymous RLS and per-panel preview flags. Several mutations and Calendar still reached live clients in a signed-in browser; the tour could also reuse a parent query cache. GuestBoundary now isolates fixture queries and blocks live API/database traffic, export/sign-out actions and OAuth linking throughout the tour session. Three component tests verify cache isolation, blocked requests and cleanup; desktop and mobile browser tests verify no live requests leave the tour even when the backend stand-in would accept them. No existing session was read or changed during verification.
7. Fixed: Work filtered dates by their original stored date, hiding yearly and monthly recurrences that started in an earlier period. It now uses the same next-occurrence calculation as Dates and updates follow-ups and dates from the shared clock. The clock also refreshes immediately after its last subscriber was idle, instead of retaining an old snapshot until the next tick. Three component tests cover reopening, timer sharing and recurrence across midnight; 15 existing date-calculation tests also pass.
8. Fixed: calendar event links now remain visible on touch screens and keyboard focus, with at least 24 px targets. Inbox capture has an explicit accessible name and rejects duplicate submissions while pending. Rendered checks remain pending for this batch.
9. Fixed: client creation now uses native form validation and explicitly associated name, website, email and notes labels. Invoice line-item labels and unit suggestions use unique React ids, including multiple rows. Eight desktop/mobile browser cases cover both languages, invalid-email rejection, duplicate ids, overflow and full WCAG scans; all pass. Two additional tour isolation cases pass on the fresh optimized build. Invoice/tax-registry unit coverage adds 52 passing tests; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.
10. Fixed: Clients groups relationships once when records change and computes invoice totals only for the expanded client. Searching no longer rescans every relationship collection for every organization or recalculates all invoice totals on each keystroke. Deleting an organization invalidates the related cached families so database foreign-key updates are reflected in other destinations. No FX, invoice arithmetic or stored snapshot semantics changed.
11. Fixed: a saved Goal is retained when optional Calendar creation or linking fails. That failure no longer leaves an apparently failed insert to repeat. A 15-second request limit and visible, bilingual confirmation warnings make the partial outcome explicit. Three component cases cover network, HTTP and link-write failure.
12. Fixed: Calendar and Dates use the day a new form opens; Calendar preserves an explicitly chosen date. Dates updates its occurrence list at local midnight and blocks duplicate pending writes. Goal timeline dates now parse as local date-only values and expose named status controls. Eight desktop/mobile browser cases in Los Angeles cover date changes, retained choices, timeline dates and clean hydration; all pass.
13. Fixed: Home and Calendar no longer render browser-local event times in the server hydration snapshot. A browser west of the server timezone previously produced a real React hydration failure, which also reset language during the failed test. Local event times now render after the shared clock hydrates; explicit console checks pass in both desktop and mobile cases.
14. Fixed: the public fixture tasks, goals, notes and calendar still advertised retired lifestyle examples (dentist, sport and chores). Their sample wording now represents release work, client follow-up, accessibility and cost reviews. Invented bookkeeping examples distinguish office supplies and office rent. These are fixture-only changes; no owner records or connected calendars were edited. A scope regression test protects the public examples.
15. Fixed: fixture entity stores no longer refetch live data after cache invalidation. Dev-preview uses the same network/cache isolation as the public tour whenever configured with a real backend; only the deliberately nonexistent E2E backend permits mocked mutation requests. Three server-boundary tests and the shared route/tour tests pass (17 cases in that targeted run).
16. Fixed: timed Calendar writes now carry the browser's IANA zone instead of using the server zone. Dates, time order, recurrence, field types and lengths are validated before a Google call. All-day reads respect exclusive midnight ends, including DST. Provider failures and missing ids cannot report a confirmed success; uncertain writes are not automatically resent. Agenda caches refresh after attempts. Upstream error bodies no longer enter Calendar telemetry. The Google contract was checked against its [Events reference](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/v3/reference/events). Calendar/weekly-planning coverage passes 53 tests, and ten desktop/mobile planning cases pass, including the submitted timezone. A cold dev-server guest navigation exceeded 90 seconds; waiting for DOM readiness and the interaction assertions produced two passing guest isolation rechecks. Full optimized release validation remains pending.

17. Fixed: Daily Focus now summarizes every saved generation within its 49-day window in one RLS-scoped database read. More than 350 regenerations cannot erase a completed day. Failed history reads remain unavailable rather than displaying invented incomplete days. Cached tasks survive refresh/regeneration failures; retrying a failed initial read does not regenerate the selection. The query changes at midnight in the stored focus timezone, including after an idle tab resumes. Four component recovery cases and migrated-database/window tests cover these boundaries. The fourth prepared migration must precede deployment.

18. Fixed: Home layout synchronization failures are visible and editing makes inner widget actions inert for both pointer and keyboard users. Recurring-plan failures report their rollback and pending toggles cannot race. Goal deletion reports a failed optional Calendar cleanup while retaining the confirmed local deletion. Preferences reject malformed bodies, invalid timezones/reminder hours and currencies outside the static FX table before any write. Unknown widget ids cannot enter the saved layout. Fourteen preference-route cases and the customization interaction check complement the existing plan failure tests.

19. Fixed: Tasks refresh now pages a stable, owner-scoped projection of existing GitHub tasks before calculating its diff. Failed, null or over-limit snapshots abort instead of importing against an invented empty list. A 1,201-row regression catches the REST-cap duplicate risk. A failed Daily Focus request cannot misreport the already-saved task sync as failed, and failed mutations refresh cache state and show a bilingual warning. Task/project matches are memoized, due chips update from the shared clock, and delete controls remain discoverable by keyboard and narrow-screen touch. Forty NEEDED parsing/sync tests pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.

20. Fixed: Project health recognizes overdue repository tasks by the shared id/name matcher, including renames. Project next dates use recurrence and the browser day rather than UTC and the original stored date. Workspace invoice totals group items once instead of rescanning every line for every invoice; subscription calculations reuse their inputs. Failed automatic repository updates no longer cause an unconditional invalidation loop. Project deletion refreshes related entity caches after database cascades or cleared links.
21. Fixed: Project-note writes are serialized, so a slow older save cannot overwrite a newer edit; a controlled delayed-write component test verifies ordering. Workspace tabs have roving keyboard focus and a named panel. Communication timestamps display browser-local time with the original instant retained in a time element. Project URLs validate before submit; expanded cron controls have accessible names and actions remain available on narrow screens and keyboard focus. Disabled repository-document integrations cannot be bypassed through the refresh button, and a failed legacy-file fallback remains an error. Four desktop/mobile browser cases in Los Angeles pass with full accessibility scans. Twenty-three targeted project/clock cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.

22. Fixed: closing project operations during the editor’s 600 ms debounce now persists the captured Markdown directly from the editor. The previous parent callback depended on an imperative ref that React clears during unmount, losing the last edit. Two real-editor browser cases verify a quick edit followed immediately by closing. Failed Markdown parsing shows an error instead of an endless skeleton. Six current project browser cases pass after this change.

23. Fixed: Career enrichment rejects malformed and credential-bearing URLs, preserves manual edits during a delayed import and cannot replace a subsequently entered URL. Failed application deletion restores its history as well as the row. Optional contact-write rejection preserves already-confirmed progress and the unsaved contact fields. Saving a draft and recording an application cannot run together. The Strong fit filter now matches its badge level (75%, formerly an inconsistent 50%). Current location guidance remains conditional on owner-confirmed facts.
24. Fixed: Career defaults, RPC future-date checks and seven-/thirty-date statistics share the saved account timezone. Calendar-date windows include today without adding an eighth or thirty-first date; a Prague response before UTC midnight counts immediately. Four migrated-database cases preserve owner isolation, idempotency and actual future-date rejection. The fifth prepared migration adds the invoker date helper. Fifty-nine targeted Career tests pass; the current prepared-application flow passes on desktop/mobile, the Czech six-width matrix passes once, and six import/date/delete recovery cases pass. The pipeline test now filters the recorded application cohort instead of relying on fixture invalidation replacing all other sample applications. Full optimized release checks remain pending.

25. Fixed: Finance expense categories no longer add estimated subscription charges on top of recorded ledger expenses. Planned recurring costs remain in Cost overview. Form dates refresh on open unless explicitly chosen, monthly windows update with the shared clock, and clock subscribers refresh immediately when the window regains focus. Failed account edits retain their named draft fields, show rollback feedback and prevent overlapping writes; deleting an account refreshes related transaction links. Fifteen finance/clock cases pass, and four desktop/mobile form/recovery checks pass with full accessibility scans of account editing. Financial completeness beyond the existing ledger/line-item row caps remains under review; this is not a claim of a complete Finance release.

26. Fixed: a subscription saved before an allocation failure is retained in the cache and form. Retry updates its confirmed id instead of inserting a duplicate. Allocation caches reconcile after partial writes, conflicting controls wait for pending mutations, and failed toggles/deletions show feedback. Subscription and Cost overview renewal labels follow the shared clock; stored dates are never advanced or inferred. Thirty-seven subscription/development-finance cases pass, including an injected renewal boundary. Two desktop/mobile browser cases prove one insert, a failed allocation and one update on retry.

27. Fixed: invoice saves now commit header and lines in one RLS-scoped transaction. A replacement-line failure rolls everything back; identical retries retain one document, while changed retries, stale snapshots and incomplete item collections are rejected. Edits preserve supplier and payment state. Native numeric/date validation blocks invalid fields, quantity zero now saves as zero, pending forms cannot change underneath a save, and item controls retain mobile target sizes. Five real migrated-database cases plus 25 existing invoice-math cases pass. Twelve desktop/mobile browser cases pass across EN/CS form validation, labels, accessibility and retained drafts/retry identity. The sixth prepared migration must precede deployment. Invoice imports, supplier settings and financial read completeness are still being reviewed.

28. Fixed: PDF imports no longer treat a gross total as a VAT base or snap mixed tax amounts to a guessed rate. Unsupported or inconsistent tax amounts stay blank for review; evidenced exact/whole-CZK rounding is preserved. Impossible extracted dates are ignored. Local PDF processing is bounded to 20 MiB, 100 pages and one million text characters, releases failed loading tasks and prevents overlapping imports. Supplier terms preserve zero days, failed saves retain fields, and controls wait for logo processing/saving. Invoice actions remain visible on touch/focus, document tables scroll within the paper, overdue labels follow the shared clock, and QR codes have an accessible name. The information token and paper labels were adjusted after a full invoice accessibility scan found insufficient contrast. Fifty import/parser/math cases and four desktop/mobile browser cases pass, covering supplier recovery, six-width document reflow, full accessibility scans and print isolation. The dev server retained an old CSS bundle until restart; the final passing run used the current information token. An initial print test incorrectly searched for a hidden toolbar through an accessibility locator; it now checks the print rule directly.

29. Initial correction, superseded by finding 35: server and in-shell financial reads page complete ordered transaction, invoice and item collections rather than stopping at a REST row cap. They count once, respect a lower provider page cap, reject missing counts, later-page failures, duplicate rows and incomplete snapshots, and commit no partial result to the cache. Small accounts retain one request per entity. Transfers remain bounded to 20,000 transactions, 10,000 invoice headers or 50,000 lines and 200 pages; exceeding these bounds is an explicit read failure, never a truncated report. Eleven reader/server-boundary cases pass, including 1,201 records and a simulated 200-row provider cap. Route-scoped loading and own-only RLS remain unchanged.

30. Fixed: opening Notes no longer deletes old rows that look empty from their title/plain-text projection. Legacy rich content can exist without either projection, and genuinely empty drafts also belong to the owner. The automatic destructive effect was removed; deletion remains an explicit confirmed owner action. A mounted-library regression includes both an old untitled rich-content note and an old empty draft, and verifies no delete or cache-removal action occurs. Notes autosave, import recovery and full interaction review continue separately.

31. Fixed: opening supplier defaults from an invoice keeps the document form mounted, so returning preserves its unsaved buyer, number and lines. New documents follow updated supplier defaults; issued edits retain their original supplier snapshot. Pending PDF extraction disables conflicting list actions so a completed parse cannot replace a newly opened invoice draft. Two desktop/mobile return-flow cases pass. The test locates the visible supplier textbox: the shell owns the sole heading, and label-only queries also include the intentionally hidden mounted invoice form.

32. Fixed: failed Markdown imports retain their text and show an inline error; they no longer close the dialog with a false success toast after the create failure was swallowed. Pending imports protect their input and cannot overlap another note creation. Search, Markdown, tag and note-link inputs have explicit accessible names; icon-only tag submission is named, tag filters expose pressed state, and tag removal meets the minimum target size. Four EN/CS desktop/mobile import recovery cases pass with complete dialog accessibility scans; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Autosave draft retention across note changes remains in progress.

33. Fixed: Notes retains an independent in-memory draft and error per note. Switching notes after failed saves cannot lose either draft. Older confirmations cannot mark newer, undebounced keystrokes saved; ordered writes coalesce identical in-flight flushes and preserve independently changed metadata. Failed pin/tag/order updates restore only their own field, and failed deletion leaves the document open. A single inline save warning replaces duplicate status/toast errors. Navigation guards register immediately, can save all drafts, and notify the editor before an explicit discard; queued and unmount-cleanup saves from discarded editors are skipped. A request already sent can still finish and its confirmed database state is retained. Drafts are never persisted to browser storage. Clicking the active destination is a no-op. Fourteen component/guard cases pass. Fourteen Notes desktop/mobile cases pass across EN/CS, recovery, metadata races and mount-only write prevention; four existing DNESKAi navigation cases also pass (the two desktop cases required a warmed dev-server recheck after cold fit/render operations exceeded the existing five-second assertion window). TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Full optimized release checks remain pending.

34. Fixed: unsupported non-array note content cannot open as an invented empty writable document. It stays intact with a localized recovery warning and an explicit original-data download; unavailable Markdown/publish actions are disabled. Note-list timestamps use the shared hydrated clock and semantic time elements. Context-copy and full Markdown-copy controls have distinct names and feedback, and exported filenames retain Czech characters with a safe fallback. Two preservation/format component cases and two desktop/mobile mount/no-write cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.

35. Fixed: financial and affected Knowledge collections now use one complete, bounded invoker/RLS JSON snapshot per entity. This replaces finding 29’s HTTP pagination, removing extra requests and cross-page drift without raising the REST row limit. Eight entity names and ordering rules are fixed in SQL, with explicit row/10 MiB bounds and no partial cache on failure. Read-only Production capacity checks returned only aggregate counts and row-JSON byte estimates: 23 transactions, 0 invoices/items, 20 prompts, 0 prompt links, 569 project links, 359 library links and 67 categories; the largest collection is about 0.55 MB. No record fields or secrets were returned and no data was changed.
36. Fixed: prompt headers/resources save in one transaction, retaining link ids and rejecting stale/incomplete source identities. Failed writes preserve the editor and retry id. Missing resource metadata no longer silently removes its association. Curated imports compare the complete owner library under a transaction lock and preserve custom prompts. Pending forms prevent conflicting changes; Curated labels replace misleading Public wording while keeping own-only RLS. Grouping runs in one pass, category matching is locale-independent, and the unused multi-request link-diff writer was removed.
37. Fixed: copy previews reset on each opening and guard against a late clipboard result closing a newer preview. Pending copies cannot change project or submit twice; failure retains the selectable text. Thirty-five targeted database/reader/composer/server cases pass, including actual migrated-database rollback, ownership, repeat imports and read bounds. Sixteen desktop/mobile browser cases pass, including two repeated curated-import cases; two duplicate width/workspace variants are intentionally skipped. Four clipboard recovery cases also pass after the final close guard. Generated read types reproduce from 93 migrations. The seventh prepared migration must precede deployment; full optimized release checks remain pending.

38. Fixed: project-link writes serialize across helper instances and reconcile after rejected or ambiguous requests. Failed optimistic edits restore only their changed fields; failed removal restores only missing target pairs. Inline notes use the editor’s captured version, keep failed drafts and submit explicitly rather than saving again on blur. Library pickers and Add to project retain selection/text on failure, prevent overlapping submission and protect pending controls. No duplicate failure toast accompanies their inline errors.
39. Fixed: Tools keeps its confirmed saved id and project-note draft after a partial link failure, so retry updates the same tool. Linking runs before removal; failed writes always release pending state. Re-adding a tool preserves its existing tool relationships, and adding an existing project relation retains its note. Status changes patch only status; failed deletion retains the form. Card project usage and costs are memoized instead of rescanned on every render, mobile inputs meet the shared height, and repository timestamps wait for hydration. Twenty-five targeted cases and 20 EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass, including picker, network rejection, note retry, partial tool saves and existing navigation. Two duplicate browser variants are intentionally skipped. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Tool header and project-link writes remain separately recoverable operations, not one database transaction.

40. Fixed: Links and IG TIPS preserve failed edits and use the version captured when their editor opened. Pending saves protect fields and dismissal. Confirmed deletions update only target rows and invalidate project links, prompt links and Tools, instead of restoring stale whole-library snapshots on failure. Category renames retain failed text; category merges now run in one invoker/RLS transaction, rejecting changed membership and rolling back moved links if deleting the source fails. The eighth audit migration is prepared, not applied.
41. Fixed: URL auto-fill sends only the submitted URL, cancels on close, times out, validates response metadata and leaves newer typed fields intact. The route rejects non-string, overlong and credential-bearing URLs before any provider call. Metadata and repository files stream only a bounded prefix and cancel the remainder; incomplete structured documents are refused. Library cards avoid repeated usage/rating text and unsafe source links. Export previews are built only when opened, prevent overlapping clipboard writes and keep failure text selectable. Cards/exports index project usage once per collection; grouped exports avoid repeated array copies. Seventy targeted cases and 30 browser cases pass across EN/CS, save recovery, atomic merge retry, clipboard/download, keyboard and the six-width card matrix with accessibility scans. Two duplicate browser variants are skipped. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; read types reproduce from all 94 migrations. All provider/network writes in these checks use fixtures.

42. Fixed: References exposes command/table edit and delete controls in normal flow, with 44 px touch targets, row-specific accessible names and no overlap with the copy target. Search has a name, narrow layouts use one command card per row, tables have keyboard-focusable named overflow regions, and section headings follow the page hierarchy. Failed saves retain the command/reference fields inline, protect pending dismissal and use the source version captured when editing began. Confirmed deletion filters only its target, avoiding stale whole-list rollback. Four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases cover command/table failure and retry, pending controls, touch sizing, keyboard access, reflow and accessibility. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. References stays hidden from primary navigation and remains available at its existing route.

43. Fixed: payment matching no longer links a transaction and marks its invoice paid in separate requests with a best-effort undo. It reads one complete owner-scoped snapshot, checks captured header/line/payment identities, and atomically settles up to 100 pairs under invoker rights. An identical retry confirms the existing pair, and the locked payment supplies the paid date. Service callers remain explicitly owner-scoped; owner discovery is service-only and cannot lose owners behind repeated invoice rows. The ninth prepared migration creates no table and changes no policy. No Production financial rows were changed.
44. Fixed: old unmatched rows no longer starve newer eligible payments behind the former first-500 read. A pass uses one snapshot, one bounded settlement RPC and one best-effort notification batch instead of a write/notification fan-out per match. Non-finite/nonpositive values cannot auto-settle, and cent comparison avoids binary floating-point errors at the existing tolerance; the existing rounding and explicit manual-override rules are unchanged. Owner controls protect concurrent matching/linking, preserve failed selections, reconcile after ambiguous writes and render unmatched rows in increments of 50. Seventy-three targeted cases and four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass. Database tests use the real 95-migration chain and include complete reads, ownership, atomic rollback, stale inputs, idempotency and runtime snapshot validation. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; generated read types reproduce from 95 migrations.

45. Fixed: CSV preparation uses SHA-256 identities for new imports and occurrence identities for repeated identical rows in one statement. Verified legacy keys remain deduplicated; conflicting legacy fields fail with a source row number instead of silently skipping or duplicating the payment. New identities include the full source note and payment symbol, while only the supported transaction fields reach the database. Import runs in confirmed batches of 250, keeps the file and draft after a later failure, and safely retries previously confirmed strong keys. Pending reads/writes protect the chooser; missing/malformed classification rules block import. Local file and parser bounds are 2 MiB/5,000 transactions.
46. Fixed: CSV parsing preserves quoted multiline fields, sniffs separators outside quotes, validates actual calendar dates and two-decimal amounts, rejects unsupported currencies instead of truncating their code, and refuses an overlong symbol instead of turning it into a different invoice reference. Note truncation keeps Unicode intact. EN/CS messages identify invalid/skipped records without exposing their values in logs. Thirty-one parser/identity cases and four language/device browser cases pass, including a 501-row partial import/retry, complete counts, pending controls, field allowlisting, unsupported-currency refusal, reflow and accessibility. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Without a bank transaction id, overlapping partial exports containing identical payments remain ambiguous; these local row fingerprints do not prove bank identity.

47. Fixed: the native render-trace check recognizes platform-specific Linux/Linux-musl `.so` and macOS `.dylib` assets while requiring the addon and library to share a platform. All eight actual production-build traces pass locally, including fonts and the two ffmpeg routes. Ten targeted packaging cases pass, covering actual JPEG encoding and positive/negative platform fixtures. Targeted ESLint passes. This is local packaging evidence; it does not prove a deployed Linux function.

48. Fixed: stored financial rules are refused in full if a condition, action, stage or enabled flag is malformed; an invalid condition can no longer be dropped to broaden a rule. CSV, bank sync and retroactive evaluation use one prepared order/ownership filter per batch, and retroactive counting evaluates each row once. Malformed request bodies and partial drafts are refused before ledger access. The bank sync refuses failed or malformed rule reads. The editor and write-integrity review remain open. Validation: 105 targeted cases across rule engine, request boundaries, CSV, provider and render-checker regressions pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The native-checker fixture environment also explicitly sets its required test mode.

49. Fixed: the rule editor preserves explicit clear actions and gives each field an explicit keep/change choice. Every condition must be complete before saving/checking. Failed writes retain the draft, pending operations protect dismissal, and update/toggle/delete use captured versions and confirmed row receipts. Retrying ambiguous creation reuses one UUID with conflict-ignore plus an exact retained-field check. Preview counts are bound to their checked draft; row actions have distinct names and the compact condition grid reflows on mobile. Eight EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass, including light/dark contexts, stale-save recovery, pending Escape protection, clear-action retention, ambiguous-create retry, six dialog widths and axe. Four CSV regression browser cases also pass. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Production aggregate-only verification found zero saved rules; no financial values or credentials were read.

50. Fixed: financial pattern matching uses pinned, dependency-free MIT `re2js` 2.9.0 instead of native backtracking. Patterns remain case-insensitive and bounded to 200 characters; unsupported syntax refuses the entire rule. The compiled-pattern LRU retains at most 32 entries. The [maintainer documentation](https://github.com/le0pard/re2js) describes the linear-time engine and syntax boundary. Tests exercise nested quantifiers on 100,000-character nonmatches, anchors, Unicode, unsupported features and repeated matching. The isolated browser engine grows from 2,320 to 48,071 gzip bytes; shared fetchers load it only for Finance, in parallel with the read. Final Next.js route-byte measurement remains pending. Validation: 101 targeted cases, 32 browser cases and 12 intentional duplicate skips pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.

Dependency release follow-up (24 October): resolved in finding 56 below. The earlier scan is retained there as baseline evidence; it is not the current dependency state.

51. Fixed: CSV and bank sync read complete financial rules and parent identities through one owner-scoped aggregate instead of capped table reads. Runtime validation rejects incomplete/foreign/duplicate responses, and execution refuses malformed rule rows. Service sync names its verified connection owner explicitly; all functions retain invoker RLS and explicit grants. The tenth audit migration is prepared, unapplied. Ninety-three targeted cases and twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass. The real 96-migration database tests include 1,201 projects/subscriptions per owner, ownership, service/anonymous boundaries and count/byte refusal. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; generated read types reproduce from all 96 files. The source fixture initially omitted required project slugs; the corrected fixture passes without weakening the migrated schema.

52. Fixed: a real database probe reproduced `42P10` for the earlier CSV upsert conflict target: its unique index is partial. The new ingestion RPC names the existing non-null predicate, validates an eleven-field allowlist, precision, dates, supported currencies and owner/parent scope, and returns confirmed inserted ids. Batches remain 250 rows/1 MiB and atomically roll back a later failure. Existing owner-edited rows stay unchanged. Earlier browser checks did not exercise PostgreSQL conflict inference; migrated-database coverage now does. Read-only Production catalog verification confirms the same partial index; no index or owner data changed. The eleventh audit migration is prepared, unapplied.
53. Fixed: bank sync no longer reads a capped ledger-id set, ignores insert/account/status errors or advances after a failed batch. Confirmed batch imports replay safely after partial failure; status/account writes require receipts and ownership filters. A missing balance preserves the existing value and cannot invent a new opening balance; missing metadata cannot invent EUR. Transient consent failure retains cron eligibility while reporting the failed attempt. Only fixed failure codes are persisted, and provider-bound fields are allowlisted. Validation: 72 targeted cases, twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile CSV/editor browser cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Tests cover a 501-row partial sync/retry, complete deduplication, unknown balances, missing receipts, ownership/grants, transaction rollback, malformed inputs and private-error redaction. Generated read types reproduce from 97 migrations.

54. Fixed: the bank sync route bounds its request, validates an explicit optional UUID, retains the intentional empty-body/all-owned path, rate-limits valid pulls and refuses malformed requests before provider access. Complete owner-filtered connection counts are required; partial passes and failed consent return failure instead of an up-to-date success. Cron refuses failed/capped expiry/connection reads, counts transient consent failure, suppresses success heartbeats and returns/logs the failed pass without private error causes. Thirty-five targeted route/cron/sync/database cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Connection/provider dialog UX is handled in findings 55 and 58.

55. Fixed: bank controls serialize visible actions, retain failed connections, reconcile caches after ambiguous writes and expose localized inline failure/retry states. Disconnect uses the shared confirmation, validates a bounded UUID request/rate budget and requires an owned/versioned delete receipt. Local removal is distinguished from confirmed upstream revocation. Mobile actions stay visible with unique names and touch targets; raw legacy errors are replaced with safe status copy. The bank/matching cards no longer span three columns inside a two-column grid; peer widths/top alignment pass at desktop breakpoints. Bank headings are shorter and the CSV instruction no longer promises an unsupported drop gesture. The complete bank-list read is capped at 50, checks exact count/identities, disables hidden SDK retries and uses a 15-second signal. Validation: 38 targeted cases, 16 EN/CS desktop/mobile bank/CSV/matching browser cases and an eight-case final copy recheck pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Checks include six widths, light/dark contexts, axe, pending controls, failure/retry and partial revocation. The fixture now exposes its count header just as the actual API does; earlier fixture/role assumptions are not counted as product failures. Provider connection/token dialogs are handled in finding 58.

56. Fixed: compatible dependency updates remove all 35 reported advisories (31 production) from the locked tree. Next.js/eslint-config-next are 16.4.3; the resolved Sentry, PDF.js, DOMPurify and Tiptap family use patched compatible releases. No force or legacy-peer-dependency override was used. A fresh `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund`, `npm audit` (all severities, zero) and complete `npm ls` (no conflicts) reproduce the result. Advisory presence alone does not establish exploitation: the [Next.js advisory](https://github.com/vercel/next.js/security/advisories/GHSA-2464-fhr4-w9xw), [Sentry advisory](https://github.com/getsentry/sentry-javascript/security/advisories/GHSA-mchm-gjx6-7h55) and [PDF.js advisory](https://github.com/mozilla/pdf.js/security/advisories/GHSA-hq66-cqwq-w95j) describe their specific prerequisites. Offline development scripts now declare their direct esbuild dependency rather than relying on Vite's incidental tree. The Vitest alias uses its module URL portably.
57. Fixed: the updated compiler's marketing execute trace included source, docs, tests and migrations despite the existing subpath imports. Scoped tracing exclusions remove build-only files and leave compiled runtime chunks, templates, all 46 fonts, kits, native image libraries and required ffmpeg routes intact. The local execute trace falls from 2,268 files/81,023,735 listed bytes to 628 files/39,374,941 bytes. These are local uncompressed file totals, not deployed Linux bundle size, latency or money saved. CI's checker now rejects build-only and outside-project paths. All eight actual build traces and 27 targeted packaging/render cases pass. Full unit validation on the patched tree passes 3,087 cases with one intentional skip; full ESLint, TypeScript and the ordinary production build pass. Browser compatibility checks pass 26 Notes/invoice cases, two real-PDF imports and six DNESKAi Lab cases across desktop/mobile. The Notes race fixture waits for its outgoing animated row before selecting the retained row; PDF selection uses the visible chooser to avoid dispatching on an unhydrated hidden input. Final app-wide release verification remains pending.

58. Fixed: the integration status includes each bank provider's picker capability; GoCardless no longer falls into the Fio token form because the flag was absent. Provider/institution responses are validated, bounded and cancellable; failed lists have explicit retries. Pending connects protect Back/Escape and release controls after failure, including 503. New consent redirects require the HTTPS GoCardless host/path from the [provider quickstart](https://docs.gocardless.com/docs/bank-account-data/quickstart-guide). Fio storage and first sync have separate receipts: after confirmed storage the input clears, a failed sync stays open with an id-only retry, and the credential is never held in React Query mutation variables. Controls have accessible labels/errors and mobile sizes. Nine contract cases and sixteen EN/CS desktop/mobile bank cases pass; the final eight-case picker recheck also covers six widths and the 503 return. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. No provider was activated and no live credential was submitted. The server credential/link/callback route review is recorded in findings 59 and 62.

59. Fixed: Fio credential storage and connection creation/reset now commit together through an invoker, service-only RPC. A per-owner transaction lock serializes repeated writes, confirmed retries retain one id, and ambiguous legacy connections are refused without deleting them. Removal is atomic and preserves imported records/other providers. Presence-read errors no longer become an invented absent token; write routes bound/validate requests, rate-limit and require receipts. Raw exception logging was removed. Six real database cases and nine route cases pass, plus TypeScript and targeted ESLint. Tests exercise storage/removal rollback, owner/provider isolation, duplicate refusal, invalid input and grants. The twelfth migration is prepared, unapplied; generated read types reproduce from all 98 files. No credential was read or written in Production.

60. Fixed: payment matching rejects overlong variable symbols rather than truncating them into another invoice's reference. Free-text extraction cannot capture the first ten digits of an eleven-digit value. An explicitly invalid stored reference cannot fall back to an unrelated note/number. Valid ten-digit and existing formatted references retain their behavior; manual owner matching remains available. Eighty-seven targeted matching/database/CSV/Fio cases and four EN/CS desktop/mobile payment recovery cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. No financial record was changed.

61. Fixed: Fio statement parsing refuses malformed/missing lists, invalid booked movements, duplicate identities, missing/unsupported currencies, impossible dates and invalid explicit references instead of silently omitting them. A malformed document cannot prove a linked consent. Documented epoch milliseconds preserve the Prague banking day; one immutable date formatter is shared. Requests have bounded response text, a timeout and no redirect forwarding. Each sync shares one promise across its three adapter calls; a new request rechecks the stored credential rather than reusing process-wide financial data or approving a replacement from an old statement. Forty-five targeted provider/parser/sync cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The [Fio manual](https://www.fio.cz/docs/cz/API_Bankovnictvi.pdf) documents both date forms, movement identities, the per-token interval and token validity. Current comments/setup notes no longer claim Fio tokens have unlimited validity. No live bank request or credential operation ran.

62. Fixed: bank consent start/list routes bound and validate input, enforce rate budgets and refuse malformed provider replies. A new consent link is returned only after the owned connection insert has a valid receipt. Callback reads are owner/reference scoped; success requires a captured-version write receipt, and stored failures use fixed codes instead of provider text. All raw exception logging was removed from these routes. Twenty-nine targeted route/flow cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Checks include CSRF/auth/size/rate boundaries, unsafe links, missing receipts, foreign/failed reads and generic failure responses. External consent creation and local storage remain separate operations; an upstream result followed by an ambiguous storage failure reports failure, not a successful link. No external consent or bank request ran.

63. Fixed: GoCardless HTTP reads have per-request timeouts, response-text bounds, response schemas and no redirect forwarding. Concurrent calls share token acquisition; a 401 permits one refresh and private error bodies are discarded. A provider session reuses its requisition only within that sync and checks the stored ownership reference/institution; metadata calls run together. Balance selection respects an evidenced account currency and cannot turn an empty string into zero. Missing/malformed booked arrays, invalid movements and duplicate ids are refused; absent bank identities no longer use a short fingerprint that could merge distinct payments. Existing stable external ids are unchanged. Optional null metadata stays optional, Unicode notes remain intact, and a failed expiry refresh preserves the last known date. The [provider API reference](https://docs.gocardless.com/docs/bank-account-data/endpoints) and [quickstart](https://docs.gocardless.com/docs/bank-account-data/quickstart-guide) were checked. Thirty-seven targeted HTTP/adapter/provider/sync cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. All network responses are fixtures; no real bank request ran. Cross-account identity conflicts and a total sync deadline still need their separate checks.

64. Fixed in the isolated test network: fake fetch observes and retains its original cancellation signal until settlement, cleans up its listener and refuses already-aborted work. The simulated Graph timeout also checks cancellation before waiting. The first full checkpoint passed 3,150 cases but timed out one media-publish reconciliation case after 300 seconds; it is not counted as a passing release run. Three focused network cases and all nine complete dry-day cases now pass together (12 cases, 16.52 seconds), with the original duplicate-publication/reconciliation assertions intact. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. This changes test emulation only, with no real provider call or publishing behavior change.

65. Fixed: the OAuth callback validates the parsed destination origin, including tab/newline/backslash normalization, and retains the validated URL object through redirect. An internal path that normalizes to `//path` cannot be reparsed as another host. Embedded credentials are refused. Callback storage and sign-out revocation logs use fixed failure codes without provider causes. Fourteen route/privacy cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; no real sign-in, revocation or token capture ran. Shared auth session/cookie behavior and provider-grant binding remain under review.

66. Fixed: manual invoice paid/unpaid changes use a captured-version, RLS-scoped transaction. Reopening releases every owned linked payment and clears match metadata atomically; failures roll back the full change and repeated confirmations do not write again. A trigger also clears match metadata when invoice deletion unlinks a retained transaction. Imported amounts and unrelated invoice fields are preserved. The UI protects pending detail/list/PDF controls, rolls back only its payment fields, applies confirmed released ids and reconciles both caches. Deletion requires the captured version and a row receipt; a failure restores the open detail. Row actions have invoice-specific accessible names and EN/CS failures distinguish conflict from unconfirmed changes. Forty-nine targeted cases (including seven real database cases), 18 invoice browser cases and six final copy cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The thirteenth migration is prepared, unapplied; read types reproduce from 99 files. The first fixture runs exposed text-alias sorting and separate-process fixture clocks; corrected checks retain amount/version assertions. No owner record was changed.

67. Fixed: retroactive rules now read a complete, bounded owner snapshot and apply a single atomic metadata plan. Rule writes share an owner lock; captured rule and full-row versions prevent stale application. Failed, suppressed or altered writes roll back together and success requires the exact changed identity set. Parent ownership and deterministic locking are checked before updates. The old REST pagination, best-effort update chunks and assumed row counts are removed. The request/evaluation/plan bounds fail in full; the editor captures the reviewed version before confirmation and provides localized conflict recovery. Fifty-seven targeted cases (including eleven real migrated-database cases) and sixteen EN/CS desktop/mobile rule/CSV browser cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Generated read types reproduce from 100 migrations. The fourteenth prepared audit migration remains unapplied; no owner financial records were changed.

68. Fixed: session refresh cookies and the installed SSR SDK’s cache headers survive both authentication redirects and repeated cookie callbacks. The proxy copies only response cookies and the three cache headers, never internal request-forwarding headers. Intentional public/self-authenticating paths use exact path boundaries. Fresh server user verification is preserved. Fourteen proxy cases cover refresh/deletion attributes, both redirects, repeated callbacks, cache prevention, unrelated path prefixes and unchanged sessions; targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. The implementation matches the current [Supabase SSR response contract](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/auth/server-side/creating-a-client?queryGroups=framework&framework=nextjs). No real login or session value was used.

69. Fixed: optional PostHog analytics now loads through a conditional import after configuration is present. The root no longer statically imports the SDK or its unused React context; configured capture/masking settings are preserved and children render immediately even after diagnostics failure. Unsupported “no banner/GDPR-friendly” comments are replaced with factual collection documentation. Three isolated compiled-component cases pass, including no-key module deferral, Strict Mode initialization and failure availability; targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. The runner’s initial CommonJS mock did not intercept the lazy entry, so the final test substitutes the SDK at compilation and never loads it. Earlier failed test attempts are not passing evidence. Public-route optimized script transfer is measured in finding 75; deployed latency and money savings are unmeasured.

70. Fixed: mobile login uses a short bilingual headline, one private-system label and less empty vertical space. The 360×800 Czech baseline placed the sign-in button at y=758.5–802.5; the revised callback-error view places it at y=526–570. The whole-page entrance fade and its Motion import are removed, so the control is visible immediately without transient faded contrast. Thrown and returned sign-in failures restore the button with localized safe feedback; provider details are not rendered. The old absolute “no AI calls” trust statement is replaced with accurate integration copy. Four recovery component cases pass; no real OAuth request is made.
71. Fixed: PWA icons have real 192×192 and 512×512 PNG responses from one deterministic existing mark, preserving `/icon`. The manifest no longer forces portrait orientation; metadata colors share centralized values matching the current semantic defaults. Preference-read failure still permits the system theme and Czech fallback. Twelve public/system browser cases pass with eight intentional duplicate-project skips: the login matrix covers EN/CS, light/dark, six widths (24 rendered states), eight axe scans, touch sizes, callback errors, overflow and console checks; two preference failures, original login checks, exact PNG dimensions and one-page dark-mode invoice print also pass. Seven combined login/analytics component cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Initial fixture and animation-related failures were corrected and are not passing evidence. Optimized release and transfer measurements remain pending.

72. Fixed: new bank-import identities include the local account and the complete provider-id SHA-256, so reused provider ids cannot silently discard another account’s payment. Existing same-account legacy rows and a previous-deployment writer remain deduplicated without rewriting existing ids or owner edits. A service-only wrapper and the existing generic import share invoker validation and an owner import lock. Parent ownership checks operate on distinct sets; confirmed identities/source fields detect suppressed or altered inserts, conflicting inputs and account reassignment. Dates validate before legacy filtering. Thirty-seven targeted cases, including eleven real bank database cases, and four EN/CS desktop/mobile CSV recovery cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The local read schema regenerates from 101 migrations. The fifteenth prepared audit migration is unapplied; no Production financial records were changed. The asynchronous processing-budget follow-up is addressed in finding 73.

73. Fixed: manual and scheduled bank processing share one 40-second asynchronous budget after manual auth/input/rate checks, inside a 60-second function limit. Provider calls, per-request service clients, credential/context/ledger queries and SDK backoff receive cancellation; one cancelled token waiter cannot cancel the shared GoCardless app-token request. Completed connection counts are retained, remaining connections count as failed, and failed passes suppress the success heartbeat. An unfinished connection’s cursor cannot advance after an aborted later batch or late receipt. The best-effort cron-log wait is bounded separately. A timer cannot preempt arbitrary synchronous JavaScript; shared explicit rule-work bounds cover bank/CSV/retroactive evaluation before writes. CSV retains the file and gives EN/CS guidance when that bound is exceeded.
74. Fixed: unchanged account balances/currencies avoid redundant writes. Missing upstream balances preserve the existing value only with the same evidenced currency; a changed currency without a new balance is refused. Duplicate provider account identities are rejected before balance writes. Validation for findings 73–74: 113 targeted cases across ten files and 28 EN/CS desktop/mobile bank-control, CSV and rule-editor browser cases pass; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Tests include the real SDK’s long Retry-After cancellation, independent service-client budgets, provider HTTP cancellation, a hanging provider, a timed-out later import batch, retained cursors and excessive CSV rules with no partial import. Initial mock-builder and type-order errors were corrected and are not passing evidence. No live bank was called and no Production records changed.

75. Measured: on comparable local optimized Next.js 16.4.3 builds, cold anonymous public-tour JavaScript body transfer falls from 579,278 to 509,424 encoded bytes (12.1%); login falls from 423,048 to 310,066 (26.7%). The installed PostHog SDK marker appears in the downloaded baseline chunk and in no downloaded chunk after the conditional import. Login also removes its whole-page Motion dependency. Both runs use the same Chromium, 1440×900, EN/light/reduced motion, absent analytics configuration and blocked nonlocal requests. Raw resources/build IDs are in `checkpoint-14-public-scripts.json` and `checkpoint-15-public-scripts.json`; `scripts/audit-browser-transfer.mjs` reproduces the method. These are public-route script-body measurements, with no owner session; they establish neither whole-page transfer nor deployed latency or money saved.

76. Fixed: native Next.js server/client instrumentation loads Sentry only with a DSN, catches optional diagnostic failures and retains technical errors with 10% sampled traces. Explicit current-SDK collection controls disable identities, headers, cookies, HTTP bodies, query parameters, database values, GraphQL/model payloads, local variables and source context. Replay is disabled. Export filters remove arbitrary extras, console/click breadcrumbs and sensitive span attributes; URL redaction covers credentials, queries, owner project paths, UUIDs and Fio path credentials. Calendar diagnostics remain fixed codes/status only and no longer statically import the SDK. The config-only build entry replaces its deprecated root export, build-plugin telemetry is disabled, and direct CSP-allowed ingestion replaces the application relay. Browser blockers may suppress delivery.
77. Fixed: configured PostHog now sends only page views/leaves with allowlisted technical properties and sanitized URLs. Automatic clicks, profiles, replay and unused remote flag configuration are disabled. Current setup/privacy docs describe these limits without claiming consent compliance. Forty-five targeted cases across four files, TypeScript, targeted ESLint, an ordinary production build, post-build types, all eight actual render traces and cold public-tour/login browser checks pass. Public scripts contain no downloaded PostHog SDK marker without configuration. Tests use compiled SDK substitutes and invented data; no provider event was sent. This checkpoint changes the collection settings preserved at finding 69; it does not claim a completed app-wide review or configured-provider delivery.

78. Fixed: failed provider/credential-status reads now propagate instead of becoming an unconfigured provider. Integration status requires complete bounded owner bank metadata, checks both private OAuth reads, validates its response and gives all metadata work a shared 12-second asynchronous deadline. Missing service configuration and malformed/capped/foreign metadata produce a private/no-store 503; confirmed absence remains disconnected. No credential value is returned. Settings and bank controls share one validated React Query status entry with a one-minute freshness window; bank changes refresh it.
79. Fixed: Settings shows localized loading, unavailable and retry states, retains previously loaded values with an explicit refresh warning, and explains the signed-out fixture state. Each bank provider appears once, replacing the duplicate aggregate bank row. Thirty-six targeted cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Eighteen EN/CS desktop/mobile bank/Settings browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate skips, including six-width reflow and axe checks. The initial component timing and intermediate callback syntax error were corrected; failed attempts are not passing evidence. Shared preference/project writes in Settings remain under review.

80. Fixed: OAuth refresh uses a persisted owner/provider reservation instead of assuming an RPC transaction lock survives a provider HTTP call. Only its live holder can atomically complete against the captured full-row version. New login grants, explicit disconnects, expired/replaced reservations and failed/altered writes cannot be overwritten by late completion. Confirmed provider-specific invalid-grant replies remove only the reserved source grant; transient/client-configuration failures retain it. A busy reservation reports unavailable, and an ambiguous/crashed rotation may still require reauthorization. The private lease table and both functions use RLS, invoker rights, empty search paths and service-only grants. The sixteenth audit migration is prepared/unapplied; it changes no existing credential on apply.
81. Fixed: GitHub, marketing-repository and Calendar callers share one verified-owner/request token lookup and one forced-refresh promise across upstream fan-out. No credential is cached across requests. Twelve parallel requests exercise one lookup; ten simultaneous 401 replies exercise one refresh and one retry per call. HTTP targets and redirects cannot forward authorization outside the provider's fixed API origin. Reads follow at most three same-origin redirects; repository commits use the canonical read path and writes are not replayed after redirects. The fetcher has a shared 25-second asynchronous processing budget and 12 seconds per actual HTTP call, combined with caller cancellation; this does not preempt synchronous code or bound earlier auth/preference queries.
82. Fixed: callback storage now requires a matching owner receipt. Provider expiry is no longer copied from the Supabase session lifetime; a rotating grant obtains its real TTL on refresh, with only one early-refresh skew. Eighty-four targeted cases across six files pass, including ten real migrated-database cases; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-six desktop/mobile Tools, marketing-calendar and planning browser cases pass, with two intentional duplicate skips. The read schema reproduces from all 102 migrations. A test-mock tuple type was corrected; failed attempts are not passing evidence. Provider-grant binding and explicit revoke/sign-out receipts remain under review; no real login, token refresh, revocation or provider write ran.

83. Fixed: callback grant storage no longer trusts `link=github`, session scope/expiry extras or editable user metadata. Current documented GitHub OAuth/user-token classes are checked against GitHub's authenticated user endpoint and matched to the server-managed linked provider id; scope metadata comes from the response. Google access tokens are introspected against the configured client audience/presenter and linked subject, with provider scopes and remaining lifetime. Unsupported provider/token classes are refused. Verification and persistence are bounded; failures leave sign-in available without a new stored grant and log fixed codes only. Callback exchange exceptions recover to login. Storage confirms its owner, credential fields, scopes, lifetime and provider identity, rather than only the row id. Sixty-nine targeted cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. No real provider call or credential operation ran. The [GitHub contract](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/apps/oauth-applications), [Google token reference](https://docs.cloud.google.com/docs/authentication/token-types#user_access_tokens) and Supabase's [identity model](https://github.com/supabase/auth/blob/master/internal/models/identity.go) support the checked boundaries. Explicit revoke/sign-out behavior still needs its separate review.

84. Fixed: explicit Google/GitHub disconnect reserves the captured grant before the provider call and confirms versioned local removal through service-only invoker RPCs. A stale/expired/replaced reservation, newer login or suppressed deletion cannot be reported as a confirmed local delete. Upstream revocation has a separate boolean; unavailable credentials, failed HTTP and timeout cannot invent its success. No credential is put in a REST filter. The explicit endpoints retain owner/origin checks, rate-limit actions and discard private causes. Revocation processing has a 20-second asynchronous budget and eight seconds upstream; earlier endpoint auth/rate reads remain separate. The seventeenth migration is prepared/unapplied and removes no existing grant on apply.
85. Fixed: callback exchange/capture shares a 20-second budget; sign-out shares 25 seconds with a shorter best-effort Google step. Scoped server clients stop late auth-cookie writes after cancellation. A failed SDK logout returns private/no-store failure instead of redirecting as if signed out. Sidebar and command palette share one in-flight request, check unsaved drafts before logout and navigate to a new login document only after its receipt. Failed actions restore localized retry feedback. Tour logout is blocked with its existing read-only message. GitHub disconnect distinguishes local-only removal, cancels late remote queries and marks remote projections disconnected while retaining local notes/tasks.

Validation for findings 84–85: 67 targeted cases across ten files pass, including six new real-database revocation cases and ten refresh regression cases against all 103 migrations. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass with no warnings. Thirty-two distinct desktop/mobile browser cases pass with six intentional duplicate skips, including the 25-destination/six-width matrix, focus/history/draft recovery, login and four final tour checks. Earlier missing test providers/server-only setup and the intermediate navigation lint warning were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. No real owner was disconnected or signed out. A concurrent upstream provider login/revoke cannot be made transactional with local storage; uncertain provider outcomes remain explicit.

86. Fixed: GitHub file reads now require a complete, bounded, canonical base64 UTF-8 blob, matching path, byte size and Git object hash. The returned blob version travels with NEEDED edits; a changed or removed file produces a conflict before a write, with GitHub's atomic SHA precondition protecting the final PUT. Arbitrary new text requires explicit replacement intent. Writes validate bounded input and the exact returned content receipt; malformed responses cannot report a saved file. Identical contents return the existing file without another commit. Client response contracts refuse incomplete receipts and unsafe links. The [GitHub Contents API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/contents) and [Git object format](https://git-scm.com/book/en/v2/Git-Internals-Git-Objects) define the checked conditions.
87. Fixed: both repository write dialogs protect pending dismissal and disable mutable fields. A failed export, connection or write retains the draft and restores localized controls; note export has a 15-second asynchronous deadline. An expired connection no longer closes the typed-file dialog. Unchanged files show “already saved” and do not alter repository activity. The shared mutation-origin check compares protocol, host and port and rejects credential-bearing origins. Ninety-six targeted cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. These include actual Radix dialog recovery and an independent documented Git hash fixture. An initial lazy-import test timing failure and lint warnings were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. No live repository was written. Repository note persistence/consumption, complete task import and final rendered verification remain under review.

88. Fixed: repository-note autosaves run in order against captured versions, confirm the returned body/metadata, coalesce identical pending flushes and keep failed or newer text outside the confirmed cache. The navigation guard can flush undebounced drafts or discard queued work. Failures retain an editable draft with a visible warning and discard control. Creation retries reuse one UUID and never replace an already-created note. Deletion waits for a versioned receipt instead of rolling back a whole collection over unrelated edits. OAuth connection navigation also respects unsaved drafts.
89. Fixed: repository notes/links and tasks use complete bounded owner reads on server and client. Publishing uses a fresh complete snapshot after flushing drafts, then consumes only the captured unchanged note versions. New/edited notes remain. Finished GitHub tasks use the same partition receipt, so a reopened/changed task survives an in-flight source-file commit. Suppressed deletions and altered note saves roll back their local transaction; upstream and multi-batch actions remain separate transactions. The eighteenth migration is prepared/unapplied, with invoker rights, own RLS, empty search paths and authenticated-only grants. It changes no existing record on apply.
90. Fixed: the embedded repository uses one centered, bounded column, removes the repeated section description, keeps note actions reachable on mobile and gives the textarea an accessible name. Keyboard Done waits for a confirmed latest draft. The real repository components can be tested through a dev/E2E option that enables endpoints only with the nonexistent fixture backend; the public tour and real-backend preview retain isolation. Seventy-four targeted cases across ten files, TypeScript, targeted ESLint and four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass. Browser coverage includes recovery, a changed note during publication, all six widths, both themes and axe. Initial query-builder/type errors were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. Read types reproduce from all 104 migrations. The final four browser checks open the repository directly and stub optional traffic reads; they do not establish tab-click behavior. An interrupted server recheck and a later mobile tab-selection timeout are excluded. A full 3,477-case run passed 3,475 with one intentional skip and one lazy-import test timing failure; the latter now waits for its result and passes its five-case file, with a full rerun pending. Repository-link writes, task import completeness and other GitHub read contracts remain under review.

91. Fixed: shared Progress now gives Radix the same finite, clamped value and maximum as its visual indicator, so assistive technology reads the actual completion state. Missing/nonfinite values remain indeterminate. The bank credential-status Retry button explicitly stays outside form submission; a typed fixture remains unchanged and neither storage nor sync runs. Eight progress component cases and five GitHub-dialog regressions pass, with TypeScript and targeted ESLint. Twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile bank picker/token cases pass, including four new retry-without-write checks, six-width dialog reflow and axe. The initial new fixture assumed only one failed read; it now holds the read unavailable until the explicit recovery step. Failed attempts are not passing evidence. No real credential or bank request ran. The [Radix Progress contract](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/progress) defines the numeric root value.

92. Fixed: both task-import controls share an atomic owner-scoped transaction, deduplicate existing source identities and confirm inserted source fields plus a complete removal partition. Refresh captures complete task versions before repository reads and removes only unchanged open nonglobal GitHub tasks whose lines left a successfully read source. Changed/completed tasks, legacy rows and still-present lines stay. Insert/delete failures and altered receipts roll back the transaction. Continuation-line assignees now survive import. The nineteenth migration is prepared/unapplied, with invoker rights, an empty search path, bounded input/output and authenticated-only grants. The two new import paths share its owner lock; older browser tabs should reload after deployment.
93. Fixed: fenced Markdown examples do not become tasks or check-off targets. A checkbox-based file whose tasks are finished no longer imports explanatory bullets. Displayed/imported counts use the same actionable parser. Missing/failed lists show a localized retry state rather than All clear; failed imports retain their list. File conflicts report the changed version and invalidate it once. Checklist actions have mobile targets and long-name wrapping. Seventy-four targeted cases across six files, TypeScript, targeted ESLint, read-schema generation from 105 migrations and eight EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass. The browser cases cover failure/retry, changed file versions, note regression, six widths and axe. Initial component mount timing, fixture-label assumptions and interrupted browser attempts are excluded from passing evidence. Total scan cancellation, fan-out/authentication reuse and repository-link writes remain under review.

94. Fixed: task refresh and the repository checklist share one bounded scan request instead of one authenticated app request per candidate file. The endpoint authenticates once and creates one owner/request provider context, runs at most four active repository reads, tries docs only after confirmed root absence, and limits actual serialized UTF-8 results to 8 MiB. Its 25-second asynchronous budget includes auth/body/rate work and provider reads; the client bounds asynchronous loading to 30 seconds and propagates caller cancellation. Request shape, repository identities, response completeness, rate and same-origin rules are checked. Timed-out, oversized and unstarted sources remain unavailable; a partial root-only 404 is never a confirmed missing list. These are orchestration bounds, not deployed latency or money measurements.
95. Fixed: the checklist parses stable shared file data once per change, keeps its cache key stable when repository activity reorders the list, retries only unavailable sources and refuses late retry data after disconnect/newer reads. Confirmed check-offs update from the verified commit receipt with no additional source request; conflicts reread only the affected file. Completed unobserved mutations expire immediately and commit results retain only small action metadata. Later provider calls in one context use its refreshed credential immediately, avoiding repeated rejected round trips, and rejected file bodies are canceled. The pinned GitHub API version remains supported through 10 March 2028 according to its [version policy](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/about-the-rest-api/api-versions). One hundred nineteen targeted cases across seven files, TypeScript, targeted ESLint and eight EN/CS desktop/mobile repository browser cases pass. Tests cover one auth/provider context, four-worker fan-out, total bytes, cancellation, per-source failures, cache identities, stale replies, source receipt reuse and provider-refresh regressions. Browser checks cover both languages, recovery, note regressions, six widths and axe. No real provider or credential operation ran. Finished-task removal loops and repository-link writes still need their separate review.

96. Fixed: finished-source cleanup shares the bounded batch reader, limits one action to 100 repositories and 60 seconds, propagates cancellation to file reads/writes and retains earlier confirmed results after disconnection. Unconfirmed/local-version-retained tasks receive an explicit EN/CS notice; unavailable refreshes no longer announce an unchanged complete scan. Repository-name notices show at most three names. Source-line removal now runs in one pass, excludes fenced examples, counts actual removed lines and preserves untouched CRLF/mixed/EOF bytes. The [synthetic local CPU comparison](needed-cleanup-cpu.json) removes 2,500 of 5,000 lines with identical output: three baseline runs take 470–511 ms and the new helper takes 1.1–1.3 ms. This is not deployed latency or a money measurement. Eighty targeted cases across six files pass, including cancellation, partial outcomes and bilingual component behavior; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Initial fixture/EOF-expectation failures were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. No live repository or owner task was changed.

97. Fixed: the bearer-token cron registry requires the configured owner and filters both current/old-slug project reads and cron reads by that owner. Complete bounded counts are checked (500 projects/5,000 crons); unavailable service configuration, partial/foreign reads and database errors return a private 503 rather than false emptiness or raw errors. Reads share a 15-second asynchronous budget. Heartbeat destinations remain server-only presence flags.
98. Fixed: cron history defaults to the documented fourteen days, uses a bounded owner query with stable ordering and declares when its latest 1,000 rows omit older history. The client validates statuses/dates/identities and bounds/cancels its request before rendering. Home displays one selected-day list, initially ten rows, with local Show more/day navigation and no extra reads. Retry/cached warnings and partial-empty copy are bilingual; the shared clock avoids hydration date mismatches and mobile navigation targets cannot shrink below 44 px. Seventy-four route/helper cases, sixteen component regressions, TypeScript, targeted ESLint and four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass. Browser checks include recovery, partial data, row limits, keyboard, six widths, both themes and axe; real fixture-backed component captures were inspected. Initial outdated empty-registry expectations and a real flex-shrinking target were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. Public-tour isolation remains intact; the new mocked-history switch is available only on dev/E2E with the nonexistent backend. No live job, email or monitor ping ran. Log ingestion and success stamping remain under review.

99. Fixed: external run reports require a valid explicit status, typed bounded fields and UUID references. Null/array/oversized reports cannot become successes. Authenticated ingestion is rate limited and has a total asynchronous deadline. Logging checks the owning cron before inserting, confirms every run field, then advances only the unchanged owner/version and verifies both timestamps before pinging the linked monitor. Missing/suppressed/altered writes are not acknowledged as recorded; a recorded run and an unconfirmed success stamp remain separate outcomes. Logging stays best effort for native jobs, and bank logging propagates its shorter budget. There is no cross-step transaction or exactly-once claim.
100. Fixed: heartbeat requests confirm HTTP success, dispose unused bodies and honor caller cancellation; a 500 is no longer reported as delivered. Cron interval estimates no longer treat date-restricted minute/hour schedules as unrestricted, reject out-of-range times and count duplicate weekdays/Sunday aliases once. Unsupported schedules retain the owner-entered coarse frequency.
