One hundred twenty-eight targeted cases across eight files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, covering source ownership, write receipts, cancellation, ingestion, existing native-bank behavior and monitoring reads. Initial bank mock argument expectations and a typed receipt-key error were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. No real email, job, database write or monitor ping ran. Renewal notification delivery remains separately under review.

101. Fixed: the existing renewal job reads only the configured owner, honors an explicit opt-out and fails before sending when preference/profile/subscription/sent-log context is unavailable or incomplete. At most 100 due subscriptions and 500 matching sent logs are accepted; the asynchronous job budget is 40 seconds and each provider request has at most ten seconds. Dates use the saved owner timezone (Prague fallback) and calendar-day arithmetic across DST. EN/CS emails keep the entered renewal date explicit and label invoice-unconfirmed amounts. No billing date, classification or amount is inferred or changed.
102. Fixed: renewal delivery validates the provider message id and confirms the local sent-log identity before counting a success. Stable event-based provider keys and local row ids avoid duplicate sends/log rows on concurrent retries; missing/suppressed/altered local receipts and provider failures are reported as failures and do not ping a healthy run. A fixed HTTPS origin, no redirect replay, bounded receipt and propagated cancellation prevent late local writes/new sends after abandonment. The [current Resend idempotency contract](https://resend.com/docs/dashboard/emails/idempotency-keys) retains provider keys for 24 hours; this is not an unlimited exactly-once guarantee or a cross-provider/database transaction. Existing sent-log reads supply the separate persisted deduplication. Eighty-one targeted cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including DST, both languages, opt-out, complete owner reads, retry identities, provider/local failures and cancellation. Initial Zod pipeline/typed fixture errors were corrected and are excluded from passing evidence. No real email, credential, subscription write or new integration rollout ran.

103. Fixed: development finance excludes future-dated expenses and unrelated subscription payments, includes legacy project-linked subscriptions and honors an explicitly recorded transaction project before a current subscription split. Subscription payments do not become one-off purchases. Future starts and passed end dates stay outside running totals and renewal emails; no stored amount/date/allocation/classification is changed. The Money view distinguishes not-started/running/ended states and retains zero-spend portfolio/client rows selected by the shared summary.
104. Fixed: allocation shares, converted monthly amounts, project cost groups and paid month totals are indexed/reused instead of rescanning full collections for each payment/project/month. A [reproducible synthetic comparison](dev-finance-benchmark.mjs) checks identical outputs against `33b5369` for 1,000 subscriptions, 2,000 allocations, 10,000 transactions, 200 projects and 1,000 manual costs. The [three raw local samples](dev-finance-cpu.json) take 60.5–182.1 ms before and 6.0–10.1 ms after. These are local CPU measurements, not deployed latency or money savings.
105. Fixed: Money waits for the local day before displaying date-sensitive totals and recomputes them once per day; project-list/workspace cost memos also refresh at a local-day change. Distinct chart/detail titles have heading semantics; tables have accessible captions, named keyboard scroll regions and mobile project-link targets. Bilingual copy distinguishes estimates using current prices/shares from historical financial records. All three financial chart components honor reduced motion, and the shell disables its padding transition under that preference. Seventy-five targeted cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Four final EN/CS desktop/mobile Money cases pass, including zero-spend client visibility, keyboard, currency changes, six widths and axe; eight repository regressions pass against the same runtime source before the final Money-only row correction. The final screenshots were inspected. Initial heading-test failures exposed missing semantics; later repository resize failures exposed an unconditional reduced-motion transition, now corrected with adaptive geometry checks. Failed attempts are excluded from passing evidence. No live financial record, email, provider request or billing integration was changed. Large-list DOM limits and remaining shared preference behavior still need review.

106. Fixed: subscribed theme, language, currency, navigation visibility/order/collapse, project-tab visibility, task counts, CV strings, Career visibility and overview layout observe local-storage changes from other tabs. One listener per observed store is removed with its last subscriber; unrelated/session-storage events are ignored, clear events reset caches and late events read the latest value. Handling an event neither writes it back nor posts a server preference. Currency/task-count values are restricted to supported options and navigation setters normalize legacy/canonical ids. Theme-toggle labels are bilingual with mobile targets, and Settings copy names Home/Settings rather than the hidden Inbox. An initial mobile case exposed a theme event arriving before the other tab subscribed. Observers now reconcile defined saved values immediately after attaching and compare storage when resuming after the last consumer unmounted; an unchanged cache preserves an in-memory fallback. Fourteen targeted cases across four files, TypeScript, targeted ESLint and all four final EN/CS desktop/mobile two-tab cases pass. Browser checks use real native storage events in both directions. The initial failed run is excluded from passing evidence. Server save-queue deadlines/receipts, initial preference reads/hydration races, repository-filter state and account/session lifecycle still need their separate review. No live preference or account was changed.

107. Fixed: preference PATCH requests use one bounded strict contract, explicit own-row filters and captured database versions. Updates confirm the owner, new timestamp and every requested field; concurrent creation uses ignore-duplicate insertion, and suppressed/conflicting writes return no success. GET and server hydration validate stored values, normalize retired navigation/widgets, use 15-second budgets and mark failed reads unavailable rather than synchronizing defaults. The client captures each queued intent, expires queued work after 30 seconds, limits each request to 20 seconds and accepts only a complete matching receipt. Validation loads on a real preference request rather than adding Zod to the initial shell. A failed Calendar preference read no longer silently loads the primary calendar. Settings distinguishes unknown/loading/retry/pending/confirmed renewal preferences and cancels background reads during saves. Career renders CV links only after HTTP(S), length, control-character and credential checks; editing strings remains possible. Seventy-seven targeted cases across seven files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Four final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with keyboard and axe, alongside six earlier Settings/cross-tab regressions against the same source; two redundant mobile reflow cases are intentionally skipped. Initial test-fixture names, a duplicate retry locator and expected HTTP-error logging caused failed attempts, excluded from passing evidence. No live preference or Calendar request was made. Remaining Calendar pagination, repository-filter behavior, project mutation receipts and broader account lifecycle require their separate review.

108. Fixed: Google Calendar event/list reads follow continuation tokens, validate bounded typed pages and use minimal field projections. Event windows share a 20-second deadline, four-calendar concurrency cap, 40-page/2,000-source-entry/8,388,608-character budget and per-page 1,048,576-character bound. Calendar lists allow 500 entries/four pages/2,097,152 characters. Repeated tokens, inconsistent ids/times, malformed pages, missing preference context or any failed selected calendar return unavailable rather than partial planning totals or a false empty state. Cancelled instances are excluded, provider event ids are namespaced by source calendar in rendered keys, unsafe event links are omitted, and invalid dates are never formatted. Verified route/root identity is reused instead of a second authentication request. Client reads validate envelopes, remain private/uncached and consume query cancellation. Primary references checked on 24 October: [Google event pagination](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/v3/reference/events/list) and [calendar-list pagination](https://developers.google.com/workspace/calendar/api/v3/reference/calendarList/list).

109. Fixed: Calendar selection keeps a separate confirmed value and captured draft, supports retry, blocks edits/dismissal while pending, enforces 20 selections and invalidates all agenda windows after a confirmed receipt. Cancel drops unsaved selection changes. Calendar form fields also freeze during submission; shell navigation protects retained drafts and successful writes clear only their captured form. Writes bind authentication/body/provider reads to a 20-second request budget, bound both bodies, preserve ambiguous-result warnings and never automatically resend a network-failed creation. A provider-created id is required; unsafe created-event links are not rendered. Large event lists initially render 50 entries with bilingual counts/Show more, heading semantics and mobile targets. Description previews use a linear scan instead of a repeated-suffix HTML regex. The final targeted run passes 102 cases across seven files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint. The final browser run passes 22 cases with four intentional mobile duplicates/skips, covering EN/CS light/dark, six widths, incremental rendering, retry/cancel/pending/confirmation states, navigation retention, keyboard, axe and the existing timezone/date regressions. Earlier React render-ref lint errors and a cancellation-test timing failure were corrected; failed attempts are excluded from passing evidence. No live Calendar read, creation or deletion was made.

110. Fixed: repository links use 15-second, own-row/version-filtered writes and complete returned-row receipts. Creation ignores a competing owner/repository row rather than replacing it; updates reuse the captured owner plus RLS and avoid the previous extra authentication lookup. Deletion changes the cache only after confirmation and removes only the captured version, preserving unrelated links, newer edits and later removals. There is no full-list optimistic rollback. The extracted editor keeps failed drafts open, freezes fields/dismissal during pending work, protects navigation and requires review when the saved link changes; reviewing a new version retains the owner's draft. Input labels, bounded popovers and shared mobile button sizes replace the unlabeled small input/icon. HTTP(S) normalization rejects credentials/control characters, and unsafe legacy links are omitted from cards while remaining repairable. The isolated repository harness now allows mocked note/link cache refreshes; public-tour isolation is unchanged. Preference receipts also compare original returned values so normalization cannot conceal a changed array, while undefined optional fields match JSON omission. Forty-eight targeted cases across four files pass, including real migrated-database ownership/concurrency checks and the preference follow-up. TypeScript/targeted ESLint and twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile link/note/task browser cases pass, with axe and existing six-width repository regressions. Formatting and accessible-name changes preserve the checked behavior. No live repository link, provider publication or owned record was written/deleted. Repository filters, remaining provider reads and broader session behavior still require review.

111. Fixed: Projects exposes the shared repository selector where automatic project sync runs. The subscribed device store validates at most 500 safe integer ids/10,000 stored characters, checks persistence by reading it back, distinguishes unknown selection from explicit Show all and observes current cross-tab values without echo writes. A new sync pass does not start without a confirmed server fallback or valid device selection. Saves report confirmed device/server copies separately; if neither can be confirmed, the draft stays open and the effective selection is preserved. Late server-only acknowledgements do not replace a newer local/cross-tab intent. Dialog pending states block edits/dismissal, navigation uses the draft guard, changed selections require reload, and saved/new draft ids missing from a refreshed provider page are retained. Show all explicitly clears the filter. The scroll region is named/focusable and the header/footer wrap with mobile targets. Sixty-eight targeted cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Four final EN/CS desktop/mobile filter cases pass with six widths, axe, real two-tab storage events, local/server/both-unconfirmed saves and retained selections. Five Home/Career browser regressions also pass with one intentional duplicate mobile skip; those were checked before the last selector-only edge-case correction. Copy describes ambiguous saves as unconfirmed. No live preference or project was changed. Full cross-device preference ordering, authentication lifecycle, and existing in-flight project-sync writes still need their separate review.

112. Fixed: the sortable Projects table, customizable Home and Career board pass React-stable ids to DndContext instead of relying on its process-wide counter. The filter's first Czech browser run exposed the server/client accessibility-reference mismatch. The corrected filter run has no hydration warnings, and the Home/Career regressions pass. Projects also names/makes its horizontal table region keyboard-focusable and enlarges mobile drag targets. Existing explicit Settings/Notes context ids are preserved. The initial failed Czech run is excluded from passing evidence; other drag announcements and mutation behavior remain part of their page reviews.

113. Fixed (server boundary): GitHub repository list/lookup routes bind verified authentication, token lookup, provider headers/body and parsing to a 20-second request budget, with private/no-store success and error replies. A list is capped at 100 validated records/2,097,152 characters and exposes a continuation flag; it no longer claims the first page is the full catalog. Scoped lookup validates paths before provider access, follows the existing safe rename handling, bounds 262,144 response characters and returns the stable identity plus validated app metadata. Only required metadata leaves the server; owner/name/URL identity agrees, web links are derived from the canonical name and malformed/duplicate records cannot become successful results. Provider exceptions/bodies are not exposed, and invisible repositories remain distinct from expired access or failed availability. Seventy-eight targeted cases across three files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Primary references checked on 24 October: [repository list/lookup](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/repos), [repository naming limits](https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/creating-and-managing-repositories/creating-a-new-repository). Client/cache consumption, scoped workspace lookup and remaining GitHub activity reads are the next phase; this server checkpoint does not claim those callers are finished. No real provider or owned record was accessed/changed.

116. Fixed (server boundary): project commits and cross-project activity use typed, bounded provider bodies, private/no-store replies and a shared 20-second authentication/read budget. Missing repositories are unavailable, not empty history; a conflict is empty only when its bounded reply matches the documented empty-repository message. Optional code-frequency statistics have a three-second/524,288-character budget, validated safe totals and no polling for 202/204/422. The active-project projection checks its exact count and typed identities before provider access, excludes archived rows, orders deterministically and discloses the recent ten-project scope. Shared/renamed repository groups use ids plus name aliases, prefer a selected parent and have four active reads at most; a rejected grant is disconnected, a partial read has explicit unavailable counts, and a wholly failed feed is an error. Ninety-nine targeted cases across three files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including cancellation, body limits, privacy projection, stale aliases, conflicting identities, count failures, partial scope and four-worker fan-out. Primary references checked on 24 October: [GitHub commits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/commits/commits), [repository statistics](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/metrics/statistics), [Octokit empty-list handling](https://github.com/octokit/handbook), [Supabase select/count](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/select). The next phase must validate client consumption, retain failed-refresh caches and show scope/retry states; this server checkpoint does not claim the UI is complete. No real provider or owned record was accessed/changed.

117. Fixed: history clients validate capped response bodies, safe commit links/versions and activity-scope consistency, propagate cancellation and distinguish missing access. Query errors preserve confirmed data and do not retry automatically. Work discloses partial/capped scope, uses one chronological list with a compact desktop metadata column and stops live history reads in ordinary previews. Project activity centers both peer cards at the repository width, adds heading semantics, bounded author labels, date semantics, 44 px mobile links and language-aware counts; code-volume text reflects the returned week count, including zero changes. Both views share one failure/retry treatment. Sixty-eight targeted cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The combined browser run passes 24 EN/CS desktop/mobile cases with two intentional duplicate-width skips; after the final long-author/localized-count layout change, all twelve history cases pass again. Checks include six widths, main-region axe, failed initial reads, retained failed refreshes, confirmed missing repositories and zero live preview requests. The Czech component captures were inspected. The first Work fixture assumed the first request would survive development Strict Mode cancellation; recorded aborted reads proved that assumption wrong. Failure now starts only after confirmed history is visible, with exactly one manual refresh request. Initial failed fixture/type assertions are excluded from passing evidence. No live provider, publication or owned record changed. Detected-tools clients, local repository notes while disconnected, project sync/mutations and the remaining app-wide release remain pending.

118. Fixed: saved repository notes/links render from their owned identities even on a cold disconnected or unavailable workspace; no private/public/language/push facts are invented. One keyed card tree preserves an in-progress editor through disconnect/reconnect, and owned edits use the same captured-version receipts without enabling publication. Explicit project ids prevent another repository's same-name records from entering the workspace. Scoped metadata accepts only the linked repository id when known, and a recent renamed catalog identity can be reused by that id. Add-note authentication checks have a 15-second action bound; disconnect acknowledgement bodies have a 20-second/4,096-character bound, release canceled streams and retain connected/owned data after failure. The general search is explicitly labelled. The isolated live-repository harness now fetches mocked owned rows rather than declaring its empty tour rows confirmed; the public tour is unchanged. Fixtures use the project's actual fixture repository id. Sixty-four targeted cases across seven files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-eight EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate-width skips; after the final deadline/error-label guards, all sixteen local-note/task/note recovery cases pass again. Checks include cold saved-note reads, same-name/id refusal, disconnected save, verified reload/reconnect, six widths and axe. No real provider, publication or owned record changed. This binds workspace metadata/control selection, not an atomic repository-id precondition for every file-write endpoint. File-dialog dirty navigation, shared pending-navigation semantics, detected-tools and project sync/mutations remain under review.

Finance follow-up: findings 72–74 address cross-account identities, asynchronous processing budgets and balance-write correctness. Integration availability failures are addressed in finding 78. Invoice payment reversal is addressed in finding 66; retroactive rule-write integrity is addressed in finding 67. Invoice draft navigation and legacy QR symbols still need review.


114. Fixed: repository clients validate bounded/cancellable responses and cache provider receipt times separately from local push-time changes. Unavailable refreshes throw at the query boundary, retaining confirmed metadata with explicit Retry. Cold workspaces resolve their linked repository directly, including renamed identities and repositories beyond the recent 100-row page; they make no catalog request. A shared disconnected result overrides older scoped metadata, cancels pending projections and preserves owned notes/links. Projects does not start a new automatic sync pass after a failed catalog refresh. Task refresh obtains a current catalog first and discloses a continuation instead of announcing a complete unchanged scan. Archived repositories retain local controls while publication is disabled. The publication picker localizes private labels, preserves confirmed commit receipts after disconnection and blocks reauthorization during a pending export. Ninety-six targeted cases across ten files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate-width skips, covering scoped lookup/retry/cache retention, archived controls, link/note/task recovery, filters, six widths, keyboard and axe. The browser run precedes the final disconnected-cache precedence adjustment, which has its own mounted regression. All data/providers are fixtures; no real publication or owned record changed. Activity/commit reads, in-flight automatic sync and cold disconnected local-note access still need separate review.

115. Fixed: cold project URLs previously server-rendered Overview before adopting `projectTab` in the browser. Pointer logging reproduced a click beginning on Overview's identically named Add link control and ending after the repository panel replaced it. Authenticated, preview and guest entry points now seed a single canonical tab through the persistent shell; client History API subscriptions remain. Ambiguous/unknown query values use the existing default. Initial-response checks with JavaScript disabled and unmodified fast-click link cases pass on desktop/mobile. Streaming SSR markup is inspected with hidden elements included; the first visible-only server-markup assertion was invalid and is excluded from passing evidence. The earlier failed/interrupted link investigations are also excluded. No arbitrary delay or repeated click was added to the interaction test. Other workspace tabs/visibility and the optimized app-wide release remain under review.

119. Fixed: the authenticated catch-all and lazy client loaders read complete project, cost-line and cron collections in one owner-scoped statement snapshot instead of silently accepting the ordinary REST row cap. All prior collection cases retain invoker RLS and authenticated-only grants. Each new collection refuses more than 20,000 rows or 10 MiB rather than displaying a partial total. The shared collection reader disables hidden retries, settles within 30 seconds and accepts caller cancellation, including a stalled transport. Thirty-eight targeted cases across five files pass, including a real upgrade, 1,201 rows per owner/table, deterministic ordering, ownership/grants and row/byte refusal. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and schema generation from 106 migrations pass. The full unit suite passes 3,954 cases with one intentional skip (394 passed files, one skipped file) in 171.15 seconds with two workers. The twentieth audit migration remains prepared/unapplied; no production records changed. Metadata, ordering, sync and form lifecycle remain under review.

120. Fixed: drafts explicitly report pending work to the shared navigation guard. Pending actions remain protected even without a dirty field; discard and duplicate save cannot abandon them. An external action allows Stay while it finishes, and the guard’s own save also prevents dismissal, hides its inactive close control and exposes busy semantics. Calendar forms/selection, Notes, repository notes/links/selection, the daily mix and the Lab register their actual pending states. Repository file drafts now register immediately, retain failed text and leave only after a confirmed write; a synchronous latch prevents duplicate submissions. Sixty-three focused cases across seven files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Four final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases cover real shell Back/restore, failed-write retention, external and guard-initiated pending states, six widths, keyboard and dialog axe; Czech mobile/desktop captures were inspected. Eighty-six Calendar/Notes/repository/Lab regression browser cases pass with four intentional duplicate-width skips before the final close-control/busy-semantics adjustment. The first new browser attempt incorrectly treated replace-state workspace tabs as new history entries; the passing setup uses actual Projects-to-workspace shell navigation. No real publication or owner operation ran. Project forms/sync/writes and the final optimized app-wide review remain pending.

121. Fixed: project dragging uses one owner-scoped transaction rather than independent row updates. Complete active-group membership and captured versions are checked before changing only required positions; suppressed/altered writes roll back the whole order. Minimal receipts omit project text, and cache application preserves unrelated edits/removals instead of restoring an earlier list. Requests disable automatic retries and have a 15-second bound; the database has a five-second lock-wait bound. Own/client groups reorder independently and child rows stay with their parent. Drag targets now explicitly disable both draggable and droppable behavior: the installed library deliberately treats a boolean disabled flag as draggable-only. Names and EN/CS instructions/role descriptions/announcements replace raw UUID feedback; handles have project-specific names, retain keyboard focus through saving, and the table has a caption. Sixteen focused client/database cases pass; 59 expanded project/navigation cases across seven files pass. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and schema generation from 107 migrations pass. Twelve final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate-width skips, including failed/stale recovery, preserved refreshed metadata, keyboard moves, blocked cross-group pointer drops, pending navigation, six widths and axe. Earlier diagnostic, transient-announcement, interrupted and immediate-geometry fixture attempts are excluded. The twenty-first migration remains prepared/unapplied. Manual metadata, cost/cron forms, notes and automatic sync remain under review.

122. Fixed: a requested exit resumes when an external action clears the last protected draft; a failed dirty draft remains on the page. Mobile error toasts previously covered the navigation bar and could prevent switching destinations indefinitely while hovered. Shared feedback now clears the app’s bottom bar through 767 px, including the library’s different 600 px breakpoint. The Work label is shorter and every primary mobile label remains on one line with its full accessible name, keeping peer controls at the same height. Notification-region and dismiss labels have EN/CS copy; close controls use 44 px mobile and 32 px desktop targets. The final browser run in finding 121 verifies real navigation while the failure notice is visible, full toast/bar geometry at 360/430/640/767 px, close/retry, toast/main-region axe and preserved drafts. Czech crops at 360/767 px were inspected. The initial mobile run exposed the real overlay and a wrapped navigation label; these were fixed rather than dismissing notices to bypass navigation. Pointer-drop checks dismiss the acknowledged notice only after proving navigation stays usable. All values/providers are fixtures; no owner write, publication or spend occurred.

123. Fixed: the registry resolves existing keys and current repository names before considering historical aliases. A row can claim only one entry; conflicting names, duplicate candidates and unknown explicit keys cannot cause key oscillation or another inferred project. Read-only categories/descriptions use the same current-name priority and refuse ambiguous history; owner summaries and the public-preview boundary remain intact. Settled plans skip history scanning. Grouping classifies each project once before sorting and shares registry order with child sorting. Thirty-seven focused cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The local [CPU comparison](portfolio-identity-cpu.json) checks exact output parity for 5,000 non-conflicting rows: median grouping falls from 7.68 to 1.83 ms across 31 alternating samples after five warmups. This is synthetic CPU evidence, not production latency or financial savings. The already-settled plan measures about 0.04 ms in both versions. An initial frozen-row assertion incorrectly required a missing optional property to exist as undefined; the corrected case keeps the immutability check and is the passing evidence. No provider or owner write ran. Automatic sync’s availability/write lifecycle, task identity is addressed in finding 124; automatic write lifecycle and the project forms remain under review.

124. Fixed: shared task association gives explicit project bindings and known repository IDs priority across all projects before legacy names. Known mismatches cannot match current/history names, and an inactive known identity cannot be reassigned to an active name-only row. Canonical stored order resolves legacy ties even after an in-place reorder. The shell builds one index for Projects, Work, Tasks and project workspaces, replacing repeated project/task scans without persisting inferred foreign keys. Task groups use stable identity keys rather than reused repository names. The additive Daily Focus function replacement applies the same priority to new generations while preserving all existing sets and task bindings. Forty-six focused cases across five files pass, including six real upgrade/owner/grant cases; TypeScript, targeted ESLint and generation from 108 SQL files pass. Eight desktop/mobile identity/reorder browser cases pass, followed by four final EN/CS identity cases extended through Work. They cover exact per-project counts, workspace contents, inactive exclusion, globals, no task writes, six widths, light/dark, reduced motion, axe and console errors. The initial database setup lacked its owner claim; browser diagnostics exposed a cache-order tie and duplicate group keys, then corrected tab/attention selectors. Interrupted and failed attempts are excluded. Twenty-two audit migrations remain prepared/unapplied; remaining source review and full optimized release/main merge are pending.

125. Fixed: Work health recomputes from the shared clock with stable data, so a task becoming overdue or an automation missing its heartbeat no longer needs a data refresh. Costs and automations are indexed by project once and shared with the project list. Date-only tasks remain due through the final millisecond of their local day. Work and the workspace share bilingual explanations, including singular/plural missed heartbeats. Before hydration, health/review windows show loading instead of guessing from a separate clock. Work's project, date, career and opportunity links now use the existing guarded shell callbacks; modified/new-tab link behavior remains with Next.js. Fifty-nine focused cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Eight identity/clock browser cases pass, then all four final EN/CS desktop/mobile clock cases pass with real midnight/heartbeat transitions, workspace parity, six widths, axe, browser Back and no server request for the four linked destinations. The first browser pass exposed the missing shell callbacks. An expanded Back test initially treated the fixture-only `/dev-preview?tab=work` path as the real `/work` route; the final run enters Work through the actual navigation control. Failed/interrupted diagnostics are excluded. Remaining planning/large-list layouts, project writes and optimized app-wide release remain pending.

126. Prepared: a project-note save contract compares the captured note's SHA-256 fingerprint under an owned row lock, preserving concurrent metadata edits. It returns explicit conflicts without writing, keeps identical retries unchanged, verifies the update/retained fields and advances timestamps monotonically. New text is limited to 1 MiB; the old document is represented by a fingerprint, and acknowledgements contain only hashes, identities and versions. The client applies only complete validated receipts, disables SDK retries, bounds waits to fifteen seconds and refuses to stamp changed/newer cached projects. Forty-nine focused cases across four files pass, including ten new real database cases covering Unicode, exact byte limits, shortening a legacy large note, grants, owner isolation, no-op/conflict behavior and suppressed/altered updates. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and offline generation from 109 migrations pass. An initial RLS test expected a custom update error even though the restrictive policy hid the row at the locking read; it now verifies rejection/unchanged state. A native timeout cannot be advanced by fake timers, so the final deadline test verifies the configured value and abort propagation with a controlled signal. Earlier failures are excluded. Editor integration, immediate draft protection and conflict recovery are the next phase; the existing autosave is not yet replaced. Twenty-three audit files remain prepared/unapplied.

127. Fixed: project-note autosave now uses the note-only receipt contract. Rich edits protect navigation immediately, before serialization; saves remain ordered, failures stop later queued writes, newer keystrokes stay dirty, and ambiguous outcomes reconcile without silently accepting unknown external note text. Conflict replacement uses its captured fingerprint only after the explicit Replace action. Reloading saved text blocks an old debounce and preserves edits made while waiting. Discard/reset generations reject obsolete editor cleanup callbacks. Oversized drafts show an actionable limit and perform no needless collection refetch. Dialog dismissal and its project-deletion action use the existing guard. Project links retain native modified-click tabs and keyboard navigation. Seventy-three focused cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The initial missing test-language export, query-observer scheduling and popup-event assumptions were corrected; failed/interrupted attempts are excluded. The source and browser counts for the final dialog phase follow below.

128. Fixed: the operations dialog no longer nests an extra card frame or shows an inert drag handle/collapse controls. Its title, totals and actions wrap by available space; long project names remain readable. Cost controls use one desktop row, an intermediate grid and three mobile rows with usable field widths and matching target heights. The currency value stays fully visible. Twenty final project browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate/mobile skips; after the last currency-width correction, ten focused dialog cases pass again with those two skips. EN/CS light/dark checks cover real failed saves, conflict replacement/reload, no private-text browser storage, keyboard dismissal, native tab gestures, unchanged-dialog exits, six widths, exact control dimensions, visible currency text, long names, non-overlap and axe. Actual captures exposed compressed content despite no page overflow; the layout was corrected and new crops inspected. Intermediate diagnostics included tooltip-owned Escape, sortable pickup measurement frames and media-query geometry before settling; final tests target the intended surface and poll the actual bounds. Full optimized release remains pending.

Repository/auth follow-up: findings 80–87 address request-scoped provider reuse, grant binding, confirmed disconnect/logout and file read/write integrity. Repository note persistence/consumption and complete task imports are addressed in findings 88–96; other GitHub reads, repository-link/manual-task writes and remaining session boundaries still need review.


129. Fixed: the active and own/client controls in Settings and Projects wait for compact confirmed receipts instead of displaying optimistic success and blindly rolling back later state. The owner-only invoker transaction checks the captured project version, returns conflicts without writing, makes identical retries a no-op and verifies every unrequested field. Requests are bounded to 128-byte JSON and fifteen seconds without SDK retries; acknowledgements omit private project text. Version comparisons preserve PostgreSQL microseconds and equivalent timezone formatting. A synchronous per-project guard prevents two same-event writes, disables both classification controls and protects pending navigation. Cache changes apply only to the exact source version; late receipts preserve newer notes and reconcile, while failures/conflicts show localized review messages without exposing backend details. Forty-one focused cases across five files pass, including eight real database cases. Ten EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate mobile skips, covering keyboard input, busy/guarded controls, no direct project PATCH, conflict/ambiguous recovery, six widths and axe. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and offline generation from 110 migrations pass. Initial tests expected a dirty-draft title for a pending-only action and used an outdated inactive-list label; both were corrected. A BigInt literal was replaced with the existing compiler-compatible constructor form. These diagnostic failures are excluded. The final combined notes/classification browser run passes eighteen cases with two intentional duplicate mobile skips. Twenty-four audit migrations remain prepared/unapplied; metadata/create/delete/sync and cost/cron writes still need their own review.


130. Fixed: an externally removed project no longer unmounts the open operations editor and loses its dirty note. The dialog retains the latest confirmed project snapshot and the same editor instance, shows a bilingual unavailable message, disables project/cost/cron actions and keeps the document read-only. New, queued and previously captured note callbacks stop when the project becomes unavailable. A dirty exit still offers Stay or an explicit discard; Save is unavailable. The read-only rich editor has an announced state, a keyboard focus target and scoped Select All so the owner can select/copy only the retained text. A successful intentional project deletion closes its own retained dialog. Fifty-eight focused cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-two final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate mobile skips, covering reconciliation removal, the exact preserved text, keyboard focus/selection, disabled actions, retained guarded exits, no cleanup write, six-width geometry, conflict recovery and axe. No new migration or owner-data operation is required. Project/workspace mutation lifecycle and the shared Markdown parser remain separate reviews.


131. Fixed: shared money rendering reuses at most five stateless formatters, one for each supported currency, without caching owner amounts or arbitrary legacy codes. Supported values retain their exact native grouping/sign/decimal output; casing/spacing normalize consistently. Malformed currency input keeps the known amount with an unknown-currency marker rather than throwing or inventing USD, and non-finite amounts render an unavailable marker. Static conversion retains its documented table/fallback behavior and no longer reads inherited object properties as exchange rates. Seven focused cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The constructor-count test uses a native Intl wrapper because Vitest's default constructor spy drops Intl internal slots; the initial diagnostic failures are excluded. [Three local CPU samples](currency-format-cpu.json) compare 10,000 invented supported-currency values with baseline `319b972`: all outputs match, with 229–305 ms before and 5–7 ms after. These are local formatter timings, not deployed latency or billing savings. The bounded cache and input rules follow the [ECMA-402 currency/NumberFormat contract](https://tc39.es/ecma402/#sec-intl.numberformat). A financial browser run passed thirteen cases and exposed one Czech mobile invoice retry occluded by its error toast; that recovery flow is under investigation and is not counted as passing.


132. Fixed: invoice payment failures now remain beside the affected list row or detail controls as an accessible inline alert. The payment action refers to that message; a retry clears it, another invoice does not inherit it, and it never enters the printed document. There is one error message and no duplicate error toast. The reproduced Czech mobile failure had the button centered at the top edge of a hover-paused bottom toast; automatic click retries alternated between that overlay and the bottom navigation. An actual diagnostic capture was inspected. Keeping the recovery message in the document removes the obstruction while preserving ordinary success notices. Nineteen focused formatter/payment/database cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. All fourteen final financial browser cases pass, including English/Czech desktop/mobile conflict and transport failures, accessible descriptions, failure retained in the detail, unchanged source version on retry, no private backend text, normal pointer retries, cleared alerts and axe. The preceding thirteen-pass/one-failure run in finding 131 is diagnostic evidence, superseded for this flow by the final pass. Payment receipt/cache concurrency and other manual invoice lifecycle checks remain pending.


133. Fixed: Competition freshness uses strict local ISO/calendar parsing so impossible dates, including invalid leap days, remain unreviewed instead of rolling into a valid day. The existing ninety-day boundary and local/DST behavior stay unchanged. Freelance links share the 2,000-character/control/credential checks while retaining their HTTPS-only rule. Notification actions reject control/overbound input, credentials and backslash/protocol-relative origin changes; genuine local paths and HTTP(S) actions remain available. Status dictionaries use own-key lookups, preventing names such as `constructor` from returning a function/object and crashing a badge. Opportunity metrics preserve submitted-only reply/win cohorts in one pass without four temporary filtered arrays. Twenty focused cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The final opportunity profile/proposal browser matrix passes once across six explicit widths, EN/CS, light/dark and axe, with one intentional duplicate mobile skip. Small static brand/GitHub/invoice badge, subscription-icon, tag-color and canonical filename helpers were also source-reviewed without speculative visual changes; their final app-wide rendered verification remains pending. The Google relink recovery and wider page lifecycle work remain open.


134. Prepared: manual project details have an owned/captured-version save contract for eight bounded fields. It keeps old slugs and same-known-id repository aliases once, clears an unrelated/unverified repository binding's aliases, validates related organization ownership and retains notes, order, active state, parent/registry and financial context. Conflicts write nothing; identical retries do not advance the version. Suppressed/altered writes roll back. Metadata-only acknowledgements omit private note text, including when an existing note is 2 MiB. The client verifies complete identities/values/history/versions, limits waits to fifteen seconds without SDK retries and applies only to an unchanged captured snapshot; late confirmations preserve newer edits or reconcile. Shared timestamp comparisons retain microseconds, and project snapshot comparisons avoid JSON-encoding private notes. Seventy-nine focused cases across six files pass, including thirteen real database and fourteen new client/history cases, plus existing note/classification recovery. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and offline generation from 111 migrations pass. A test-only unused destructuring warning was corrected; the final lint is clean. Twenty-five audit migrations remain prepared/unapplied. The manual form/create/deletion and repository/registry sync writers still need integration and validation; this contract alone does not mark them fixed.


135. Fixed: the manual project edit form now uses the captured-source metadata contract from finding 134. Form state and lifecycle live in a focused hook instead of the large panel. Failed saves and unavailable repository checks retain raw fields; conflicts require an explicit guarded reload. A background refresh cannot overwrite the captured source or draft. Removed projects keep the form readable and refuse further writes. Immediate edits and pending requests protect close, Escape and navigation; same-event duplicate saves are refused. Delayed reads cannot reopen a closed form, and unmount aborts the request and ignores late acknowledgements. Metadata requests exclude private notes. Explicit fields retain their length checks, automatic slugs fit the 100-character contract, and safe URLs canonicalize consistently. Eighty-seven targeted cases across seven files pass, including seven mounted form cases and fifteen metadata-client cases. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The final combined metadata/notes/classification browser run passes 34 cases with two intentional duplicate skips; four additional conflict cases pass with accessibility checks before reload. Checks cover EN/CS desktop/mobile, failure/retry, verified repository rename history, six widths, retained notes, guarded discard and no direct metadata PATCH. Actual Czech desktop/mobile conflict captures were inspected and added to the working visual plan. An earlier unstable pending-dialog title locator was corrected; its diagnostic failures are excluded. New project creation still uses the legacy insert; its idempotence/receipt handling, deletion, cost/cron CRUD and automatic synchronization remain separate pending work. Twenty-five audit migrations remain unapplied.


136. Fixed: project deletion filters the owned captured version and requires exactly one matching identity/version receipt. Empty, suppressed, foreign, changed or overbroad responses cannot clear the cache or report success. Requests have a fifteen-second deadline, no SDK retries and no private text. The confirmation checks the current complete snapshot before writing; pending actions disable conflicting project controls and protect navigation. A synchronous repeat guard runs before navigation, preventing a second delete from being queued and replayed after the first. Failed/lost responses show safe bilingual inline feedback, retain the operations view and reconcile every affected collection instead of restoring stale data. Unmount cancels work and ignores late callbacks. Twenty-two focused cases across four files pass, including four actual migrated-database deletion/RLS/cascade/suppression cases and six mounted lifecycle cases. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The final combined deletion/edit/notes/classification browser run passes 46 cases with two intentional duplicate mobile skips, across EN/CS desktop/mobile. It covers cancelled confirmation, pending exit, stale/suppressed results, failure/retry, ambiguous committed removal, retained readable notes and axe. Initial database fixtures used a missing clock and wrong cost-column name; those were corrected. A mounted test exposed the real queued-repeat bug described above; diagnostic failures are excluded from passing evidence. No migration was added; all twenty-five prepared audit migrations remain unapplied. Creation, automatic sync and cost/cron writes remain under review.


137. Fixed: the project onboarding guide now follows the destination repository's authorization/release rules instead of ordering an unconditional push, merge and branch deletion every session. Existing owner authorization remains valid, migrations/checks precede production merges, and unrelated branches stay protected. The English root document matches its in-app source exactly. The Projects guide has a complete Czech counterpart, including the same authorization and required-file rules. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and a source/root equality check pass. Actual EN/light and CS/dark expanded guides pass the six-width reflow, axe and console checks. The first browser attempt used a guessed Czech summary label; the corrected actual label passes in both languages. These checks made no deployment or branch deletion. The shared Markdown renderer's heading/table semantics, link validation and malformed-input parsing remain under review.


138. Fixed: Google relinking catches rejected/returned failures, prevents same-event duplicate preparation and gives one bilingual announced error associated with its retry button. Existing drafts are guarded before OAuth starts. Preparation uses the installed SDK's `skipBrowserRedirect` option, a fifteen-second deadline and caller cancellation; timed-out or unmounted work cannot redirect later. Navigation validates the configured auth origin/path, provider and callback, rejects credentials/fragments/overbound URLs and preserves Calendar/offline/consent scopes. Raw provider details never enter visible copy or logs. Fifteen focused cases across three files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Ten final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate mobile skips, covering actual rejected/hung PKCE preparation, late completion, explicit retry to an intercepted fixture endpoint, unmount, keyboard recovery, six widths and axe. Twelve existing Calendar browser cases also pass, with four duplicate mobile skips. The first new browser setup retained seeded Calendar events and never reached relinking; saving the fixture picker correctly invalidates that cache and the final cases pass. No real provider login, grant or token was used. The [Supabase OAuth reference](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/auth-signinwithoauth) and installed SDK implementation were checked; the reference's optional browser token-storage example is not used. GitHub connection initiation and the remaining broader session review remain separate work.


139. Fixed: repository documents and the project guide expose actual nested headings, named keyboard-scrollable code/table regions and column headers. HTTP(S) links share the bounded credential/control checks; long raw text wraps without widening the app. Link parsing no longer rescans the remainder of an unclosed bracket run at every opening marker. Six renderer cases preserve formatting, escaped HTML, lists, empty cells, malformed runs and visible text. The two repository document views now use the same centered 768 px reading column, remove the duplicate inner frame and stack the mobile header so a long refresh label cannot squeeze its description into a narrow column. Query cancellation reaches file reads and prevents a late legacy fallback after leaving the workspace. Twenty-six focused cases across three files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including four mounted cancellation/repository-change cases. Ten final EN/CS desktop/mobile document/workspace browser cases pass; all three document tabs cover six-width geometry, centered widths, safe links, full-width mobile descriptions, heading hierarchy, native keyboard scrolling and axe. Both expanded guide languages also pass their width/axe/console recheck. Actual Czech captures were inspected; that inspection exposed the narrow mobile description and led to the stacked header. One intermediate native-arrow assertion failed during repeated viewport changes; the final checks exercise keyboard scrolling after the width sequence. The older invalid-project-form check now verifies the disabled action and explicitly discards its guarded draft before continuing. [Three local warmed CPU samples](markdown-render-cpu.json) retain identical fixture text: rendering 30,000 unclosed brackets takes 442–787 ms before and 0.34–0.64 ms after. These are synthetic local renderer timings, not deployed latency or cost savings. The initial benchmark's synchronous plugin API was corrected to the supported asynchronous build. The separate rich-text Markdown parser and project workspace tab visibility still require review.


140. Fixed: project deep links and width changes keep the selected workspace tab inside its horizontal strip. Selection/translation/visibility changes and a scoped ResizeObserver reveal only that strip; no focus or vertical scrolling is used, and cleanup disconnects the observer. The mobile document captures exposed the previous selected tab outside the visible labels. Ten combined EN/CS desktop/mobile tab/workspace browser cases pass, followed by four focused cases that also assert the actual content scroller stays unchanged. Checks cover initial later-tab links, six widths and return to narrow, History API selection, focus retained in the panel, independent Home/End/Enter navigation, reflow and axe. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The broader workspace financial projections, links and related-record rendering still require their final source review.


141. Fixed: the public tour no longer displays owner CV/Career device preferences or legacy Opportunities loading counts. Its personal settings use one separate in-memory provider available before the first render; two mounted consumers share tour edits, and a new tour starts empty. Owner caches/storage are neither read by those tour fields nor changed by their edits. Loading metadata uses explicit owner/preview namespaces, ignores legacy unscoped records, and accepts only canonical timestamps, twenty bounded keys and nonnegative safe-integer counts within a 4 KiB stored record. Resume/storage events reconcile without writes. The guest notice's empty full-width layer no longer intercepts sidebar actions; the notice clears mobile navigation through the app's 767 px breakpoint, exposes full copy and uses 44/32 px mobile/desktop actions. Its sign-in link disables route prefetch. Seventeen focused cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Eight final EN/CS desktop/mobile public-tour browser cases pass, covering unchanged invented owner URLs, shared/discarded tour edits, owner/preview count separation, zero live API/database/sign-out requests, pointer navigation, eight width/breakpoint checks and notice axe. Actual Czech notice crops were inspected. Reproduction used the ordinary optimized fixture build, not an owner's account. Early attempts sampled lazy panels before they appeared or clicked Czech server markup before the guest boundary hydrated; final tests wait for the existing boundary marker and actual fields. A blocked-storage test cleanup and render-time test-probe assignment were also corrected without suppressing lint. These diagnostic attempts are excluded. Broader authenticated-device preference hydration/account scoping and Opportunities mutation recovery remain under review.


142. Fixed: server-rendered shell controls remain visibly busy and inert until client hydration enables their handlers. The rendered layout stays in place; a separate localized status remains accessible while the app is dimmed. Readiness uses consistent server/client snapshots, adds no timer or request, and does not conditionally replace the persistent destinations. This addresses the lost first click exposed by the default-Czech task test. Four paused-script browser cases check actual server-only markup, native focus/hit-test refusal, busy/opacity states, six-width reflow and one normal form-opening click after readiness. Eight existing Career/Home interaction cases pass with them; the four task-bound cases in that same sixteen-pass run are recorded separately in the next finding. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The Czech invoice remains one isolated dark-mode A4 page (one passing case, one intentional duplicate mobile skip). Actual initial desktop and ready mobile captures were inspected. Early test assumptions used visible text on an icon button and attempted a paused-document screenshot/route cleanup that stalled; final checks use a visible native control, capture after readiness and do not wait indefinitely in cleanup. Those diagnostic runs are excluded. The implementation follows [React's server snapshot contract](https://react.dev/reference/react/useSyncExternalStore#adding-support-for-server-rendering) and [the HTML inert-subtree model](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/interaction.html#inert-subtrees), including a visible busy treatment. Full optimized app-wide interaction review remains pending.


143. Fixed: imported task estimates respect the existing 1–100,000-minute database range. Oversized, zero, fractional or non-finite markers become an absent optional estimate while retaining the task, work kind and exact raw source line; they cannot make the whole atomic import fail. Parsing bounds marker work, formatting accepts only the same whole-minute domain, and the manual form shares that limit/step and checks before authentication or writing. Dashboard reorder helpers refuse fractional/non-finite indices instead of reinterpreting them as another widget. Career letter links use the common length/control/credential checks, reject whitespace-only stored links, and cannot imply readiness from an invalid URL; an explicitly ready text-only draft still works. Progress labels use owned dictionary keys and shared static maps, so inherited object names never turn into function/object text. Seventy-six focused cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; the unchanged legacy category-rule bridge was also source-reviewed and tested. Four final EN/CS desktop/mobile task cases cover native lower/upper/integer validation, retained drafts and zero writes; six Career recovery cases and two Home customization cases pass with them. Those twelve cases also pass in the sixteen-case run with finding 142. The first marker test used the wrong parsed-property name and was corrected. A preceding Czech click raced hydration; finding 142 fixes that product boundary rather than hiding it with repeated clicks. No migration or owner-data rewrite was added. Confirmed manual task mutations, the remaining Career sources and financial category writes remain separate work.


144. Fixed: marketing upload admission has one five-second whole-body deadline in addition to each route's byte cap. Client disconnects cancel the stream, slow chunks cannot reset the budget, and even a stalled producer cancellation cannot delay refusal. Reader locks and timers are released. Strict UTF-8 decoding preserves exact valid signed text, including a BOM, and refuses malformed bytes rather than authenticating replacement characters. Worker queue/execution admission reuses this boundary. Automation and worker endpoints return 408 for a body deadline and retain 413 for excess bytes; fixed error codes never expose producer errors or abort reasons. Fifty-two focused cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Six real route-admission cases prove stalled/oversized requests do not reach authentication, database creation, source ingestion or revision saving. Other cases cover split Unicode, exact bounds, invalid limits, empty bodies, cancellation, slow chunks and worker error compatibility. No provider, storage, publication or owner-data request ran. The wider marketing state/mutation validation, provider execution and persistence review remain pending.


145. Fixed: an unreadable enabled DNESKAi daily mix no longer restores the default posting volume. Only a confirmed absent row uses the default; saved zero-post settings remain zero, and errors/malformed rows stop the scheduling steps that read them. The owner GET reports unavailable. Prepared migration 112 adds an owned captured-version save with invoker RLS, a project lock for simultaneous first saves, exact microsecond timestamps, narrow persisted receipts and identical no-op retries. Input shape, distinct known slots, capacity and 2 KiB bounds are checked; a suppressed write cannot return success. The API validates receipt owner/project/value/version relationships rather than acknowledging submitted values alone. The browser applies twenty-second request/body bounds, a 4 KiB response cap and caller cancellation. Dirty forms survive background changes, failures and disablement; newer cache data is retained. Immediate repeat submits are refused, pending navigation is guarded, unmount aborts late callbacks, and a changed version needs an explicit discard/reload. Recovery copy is bilingual, announced and not duplicated. Thirty-eight focused cases across four files pass, including eight isolated database cases, thirteen client cases and eight mounted form cases; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. All 112 migrations reproduce for the offline generated read schema. Twelve final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass, covering pending/failed saves, native exit guards, conflicts, explicit reload, unchanged offline preview, six widths, control sizes, axe and console. Actual Czech desktop/mobile conflict captures were inspected. Initial tests were corrected to await query notifications, avoid accessibility-role queries behind an open modal and poll responsive geometry after resize; those diagnostic failures are excluded. The existing fake-backend project preview now exercises only the real mix settings while keeping other marketing actions in preview. No real provider, publication or owner-data request ran. Twenty-six audit migrations remain prepared/unapplied (112 local files). Source-catalog fallback, source edit persistence and the remaining marketing execution review remain separate work.


146. Fixed: enabled owner source settings cannot fall back to the registry after a failed read or removal of every source. Complete catalogs use owner/project filters, deterministic ordering, an overflow sentinel, exact filtered counts, all-row validation, unique identities and a fifteen-second no-retry deadline. A malformed or server-capped result is unavailable. An empty catalog remains empty and refuses new collection admission before any database write/provider work. Registry lookup cannot restore removed/disabled sources in already admitted runs; inactive education IDs do not enter new scouting reads. The source-list GET no longer seeds or otherwise writes on a visit. Its history query uses only current source IDs, verifies count/shape, skips the query entirely for an empty list and reports unavailable instead of fabricating never-read metadata. Timestamp bounds prevent malformed history from crashing display. One hundred eight focused cases across nine files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The isolated migrated-database path removes all synthetic sources through owner RPCs and proves subsequent reads remain empty and collection makes no writes or provider calls; a new boundary file also proves unavailable/empty/capped/malformed/duplicate catalogs refuse admission. The test PostgREST emulator now returns exact filtered counts for these real SDK reads. Four EN/CS desktop/mobile source-preview browser cases pass with project scoping, offline/read-only behavior, account/recovery navigation, reflow, axe and console checks. No new migration or real provider/owner-data call was added. Source edits still need confirmed persistence, retained drafts, cancellation and repeat guards, and address parsing remains under review. The larger collection/stage execution review remains partial.


147. Fixed: upload refusals have plain EN/CS explanations and recovery steps, alongside explicit empty-source, source-history and mix-save/conflict codes. Account recovery points to the current project menu. Removed-source copy also covers a source switched off during a run. Shared source-code lookup now accepts only owned dictionary keys, preventing a value such as `constructor` from becoming a function instead of readable text. Six focused code-coverage/localization cases, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. The first checkpoint-61 full run at `420efee` reported four missing upload-code messages: 4,226 other cases passed and one was intentionally skipped; that run is not a passing checkpoint. Current setup/scheduling docs also describe read-only source visits, preserved empty catalogs and captured daily-mix deployment prerequisites, and the audit index now links the source ledger instead of growing an unmaintainable feature list. Full code/dictionary reconciliation and the next full validation remain in progress.


148. Fixed: the direct pilot helper rejects a non-finite clock before comparing an active Preview window. Previously both comparisons with an invalid Date were false, so a direct caller could receive the tuple. Absent/retired non-Preview families retain ordinary non-pilot admission, and the existing request authenticators already reject invalid clocks separately. Forty-four focused cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including the new direct-helper case and the existing real local pilot reservation/ownership/expiry checks. Publisher labels were also traced into the internal model evidence offer; comments now describe actor attribution instead of forbidden post source lines/picture credits. No generated caption, slide, provider call, paid action or migration changed. The larger generation modules remain pending rather than being promoted by comment edits.


149. Fixed: source detection distinguishes a bare website such as `example.com` from an explicit dotted Instagram handle such as `@meta.ai`. A protocol-less Instagram profile address resolves as a profile. Instagram URLs must contain one actual profile segment; Reel/post/login/deeper paths, credentials, unexpected ports, non-web schemes, controls, backslashes and oversized input are refused rather than guessed or partially reinterpreted. Website input keeps HTTPS normalization and fragment removal, and its encoded result is bounded too. Stored source rows require a parseable credential-free HTTPS address on the default port, distinct weekdays and an already-trimmed bounded name without controls. Fifty focused cases across four files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including two mounted EN/CS checks of the visible detection hints without writes. The helper text explains @ for dotted handles. These browser-safe input checks do not replace the collector's DNS/pinned-fetch network protections. Source mutation receipts, request/lifecycle guards and retained drafts remain pending. No migration, provider call or owner-data request was added.


150. Prepared and verified: migration 113 adds captured source-setting save/removal receipts. The transaction locks the owned project before its source row, validates an exact bounded settings shape and returns only owner/project/source identity plus persisted settings. Changed captures return a conflict without a write; identical saves and already-absent removals are no-ops. Kind/address are immutable. Existing save/remove/explicit-seed functions remain compatible and share the project-first lock, so new/legacy creations use one 120-source limit. Authenticated keeps SELECT-only table access; the narrowly owned mutation RPC is necessarily a definer with explicit owner predicates, empty search path, five-second lock waits and grants reset before authenticated-only execution. Its private row encoder has no application-role execution. Eight new isolated database cases and the five existing full-chain source cases pass; the final focused run passes 83 cases across six files, including migration inventory/grant checks. TypeScript, targeted ESLint and offline generation from all 113 migrations pass. Initial runs exposed old constraint-message expectations, missing migration documentation and the repository checker's requirement for separate explicit revoke declarations; those were corrected and excluded from passing evidence. The browser receipt shape is prepared, but POST/client/editor integration is still in progress and this is not a finished source-edit fix. Twenty-seven audit files remain prepared/unapplied (113 local files); no owner data or provider was touched.


151. Fixed at the request boundary: source writes send the captured settings (null only for a new source) and require a correlated persisted receipt. Shared API/browser validation checks project/source identity, action, fields and meaningful conflicts; the API additionally binds the verified owner. Empty/legacy/altered responses cannot produce success. The browser clones intent before sending, accepts only known refusal codes, bounds list/receipt/error bodies, cancels and applies one twenty-second deadline with no request or mutation retries. The API uses the new transaction, 16 KiB upload limit, five-second upload deadline and one eighteen-second budget through auth, project checks, reads and writes. Parent expiry cancels upload readers; stalled uploads are retryable, unavailable auth/project reads remain unavailable, and only confirmed missing/foreign projects return 404. The project helper preserves owned filters and distinguishes failed reads from absence. Dictionary lookup accepts owned keys only, so an unknown code cannot become a function in the UI. Ambiguous mutations invalidate the canonical list. Ninety-two focused cases across eight files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass; the eight mounted source cases also pass after explicitly disabling mutation retries. Tests cover API/receipt identity failures, conflicts/removals, unknown codes, oversized/stalled responses, request/parent deadlines, disconnected uploads, auth/project refusals, real helper cancellation and the existing migrated source/scheduler paths. The initial focused run found one missing global invalid-source explanation, which was added; it is excluded. Source toggles and dialogs now pass the row they captured rather than adopting a later refresh, but Add/Edit draft retention, synchronous repeat guards, pending exits, unmount lifecycle and explicit conflict reload are still in progress. No provider, publication, migration or owner-data write ran.


152. Fixed: source Add/Edit forms keep failed drafts and raw invalid numbers instead of clearing, rounding or substituting submitted values. Background failures, disablement, removals and changed saved settings preserve the captured edit; a fresh successful editable read is required before an explicit discard/reload replaces it. Immediate repeat writes are refused, pending exits cannot discard active work, unmount aborts requests and late callbacks cannot change the cache. Removal retains its capture across confirmation. Normal confirmed changes patch only the unchanged complete query snapshot, avoiding a second whole-list GET; ambiguous outcomes refetch. Structural sharing is disabled for this query so later complete reads remain distinguishable even after values change back. Older in-flight reads are cancelled before receipt application. Notices store codes, remain bilingual and appear beside the originating form rather than behind its modal. Visible duplicate source headings/Instagram handles are removed, topic labels now describe News/Education accurately, website addresses remain distinguishable, and historical errors use plain recovery text with collapsed technical details. Feed addresses ending in .xml are recognized even with a query string. Fifty-three focused cases across three files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty final EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass across source and daily-mix settings, including captured removals, failed/pending navigation, raw validation, conflict reload/refusal, six widths, native form control sizes, axe, console and confirmed-write GET counts. Actual Czech desktop/mobile conflict captures were inspected. The initial browser fixture missed query-free POST requests; a later run overlapped the copy edit and used old expected strings. Both diagnostic runs are excluded. The fake-backend project preview now exercises source-list and mix settings only; ordinary preview remains offline and other marketing actions keep preview protection. Migration 113 is still prepared, with all 27 audit files unapplied. No provider, publication or owner-data request ran.


153. Fixed: shared switches and checkboxes use actual 44px mobile / 32px desktop pointer targets while retaining their compact 36×20 track and 16px checkbox mark. The Radix button, forwarded ref, controlled/uncontrolled state, associated label and native hidden form input remain intact. Disabled controls do not activate or enter submission; mixed checkboxes show a dash, become selected on activation and retain native reset behavior. Topic rows reserve the same selection width across proposed/reviewed states. Fifty focused cases across eight files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-eight final browser cases pass across EN/CS desktop/mobile Settings, Tasks, finance rules and exports, including target-edge clicks, Space/Enter behavior, six-width geometry/reflow, retained rule drafts, captured writes, clipboard recovery and axe. Twenty-four source/mix/ordinary-preview browser cases also pass after the control change. Actual Czech desktop/mobile Settings and Task captures were inspected. The initial measurement callback omitted its browser argument; one follow-up used an immediate count before settled Settings rendering. Those diagnostic failures are excluded. The first mounted mixed-state assertion also incorrectly assumed an initialized property on the hidden input; the final test verifies the public mixed state and actual submission/reset behavior. Task nested-card density, broad Settings/Tasks workflows and final optimized release checks remain separate unfinished work. Primary references checked on 24 October: [Radix checkbox](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/checkbox), [Radix switch](https://www.radix-ui.com/primitives/docs/components/switch), and [WCAG target-size minimum](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/target-size-minimum). The project target contract is stricter than the WCAG minimum. No provider or owner-data write ran.


154. Fixed: creating a Music-library track no longer deletes its stored object after a failed, duplicate or ambiguous metadata insert. The creation helper clones validated intent before any await and compares persisted owner-scoped identity, exact metadata, deduplicated mood order and canonical file path before success. An identical retry reuses the stored track without another file download or probe and preserves a later disable. Concurrent identical creates and a committed insert with a lost acknowledgement reconcile to one stored row; different metadata/file identity, suppressed writes and altered fields/duration cannot produce success. Invalid unrecorded uploads are retained for reconciliation rather than removed from a request that may overlap another writer. Blob size is checked before copying its bytes, and non-integral/non-finite probe durations cannot reach the insert. Fifty-nine focused cases across five files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Ten new cases run the actual Supabase SDK against all 113 local migrations and private fixture Storage, including foreign-owner isolation, simultaneous creates, lost acknowledgements, altered/suppressed writes, input mutation and HTTP download counts. The first red run reproduced four failures in five cases; a later request-count assertion was corrected to include the installed SDK's authenticated object endpoint without its optional path segment. Neither diagnostic run is passing evidence. The existing native ffmpeg fixture also passes. No new migration, owner file, real provider or production record was touched. The larger audio source review remains partial: upload/probe/request deadlines, strict complete list reads, browser receipt/lifecycle guards and a safe unrecorded-upload retention policy remain to be completed. Primary contract checked on 24 October: [Supabase inserts and returned rows](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/insert), [private downloads](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/storage-from-download), and [signed upload URLs](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/storage-from-createsigneduploadurl).


155. Fixed: the audio probe has a fifteen-second process deadline and caller cancellation with SIGKILL, waits for child close before removing temporary files, and never trusts plausible partial output from a stalled/failed process. It bounds input size before file work, stderr to 8,000 characters, analysis to five seconds of media, the probe buffer to 1 MiB, streams to eight, decoder threads to one and any individual ffmpeg allocation to 64 MiB. These are separate limits, not a claimed total process-memory ceiling. Only supported audio containers and local file/pipe protocols are allowed; one audio second must decode successfully before the duration is accepted. A real MP3 fixture also reproduced title metadata such as “Duration: 00:10:00.00” being mistaken for the measured duration. Parsing now requires the tool's own line prefixes and valid minute/second ranges. Decoder availability/timeout failures remain unavailable rather than being mislabeled as a confirmed invalid file; no metadata write or file deletion follows them. Seventy focused cases across six files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Eleven new real-process cases cover MP3, WAV, Ogg, AAC and M4A, forged title metadata, caller abort with a private reason, missing executables, input bounds, unsupported playlists and a stalled executable that ignores SIGTERM; terminated fixture PIDs are confirmed stopped. The first short fixture deadline expired before its startup marker and is excluded, as is the deliberate metadata red case.
