The final process fixture starts predictably and uses a 1.5-second test deadline; the production ceiling stays fifteen seconds. Whole HTTP/auth/storage/database/client budgets, upload draft/retry lifecycle, strict audio list reads and unrecorded-upload retention remain unfinished. Primary references checked on 24 October: [Node child-process timeout and cancellation](https://nodejs.org/api/child_process.html), [FFmpeg format limits](https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-formats.html), and [protocol allowlists](https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg-protocols.html). No provider, production data or owner file was accessed or changed.


156. Fixed at the browser request boundary: Music reads/toggles have one twenty-second header/body budget, and prepare → file transfer → creation shares one fifty-five-second budget and caller cancellation. Lists, acknowledgements and errors have explicit text bounds; POST bodies are capped at 8 KiB UTF-8, with oversized metadata refused before preparing or transferring a file. Only strict known refusal codes reach dictionaries. A 404 hides Music only when its body confirms the feature is off; unknown/inherited codes, malformed replies, duplicate identities, cyclic/skipped continuation offsets and mismatched selected tracks are unavailable. Input file types use owned keys rather than inherited properties. Captured metadata survives caller mutation, prepared file names must match the upload identity/type, and the created track must match the captured identity/metadata. A later disable remains valid and success copy no longer falsely claims rotation is enabled. Source, preview and cover URLs reject credentials, controls, backslashes and non-HTTPS schemes; timestamps are length-bounded. A dedicated cancellable Storage-only SDK transport starts no extra Auth session/listener, keeps owner cookies unchanged, remains blocked in the guest tour and never puts cancellation on the shared browser client. The already-installed Storage SDK is now an explicit exact dependency; all three Supabase package versions are pinned without changing their installed versions or unrelated lock entries. One hundred nineteen targeted cases across eight files, TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass across guest isolation, offline Music/Sources and fixture Lab version/hash transitions. The initial mounted page fixtures used a 200 offset after one row and were corrected to a consistent one-row page; a cancellation assertion was corrected to respect DOMException's standard legacy numeric code. Those diagnostic failures are excluded. No owner file, publication, provider or production record was touched. Captured server toggle receipts, complete server/infinite-page reads, upload editor/retry lifecycle and unrecorded-upload retention remain unfinished. Dependency inspection also found pre-existing Happy DOM/Undici advisories; their upstream fixes and the bundled runtime are the next review phase, not part of this passing request checkpoint.


157. Patched the affected dependency tree and aligned runtime validation with CI. Happy DOM moves from 20.14.5 to the exact 21.0.4 security release; Cheerio’s compatible Undici dependency moves from 7.30.0 to 7.30.1 without a broad dependency update. Both the complete and production-only npm advisory scans report zero known advisories after a clean install. The previous local Node 26.8.2 bundled an affected HTTP library independently of npm. Validation now uses the official Apple Silicon Node 22.23.4 archive, verified against the release SHA-256, with bundled Undici 6.29.1; the owner’s global toolchain is unchanged. `.nvmrc` pins the patched Node 22 LTS release and both CI jobs read it. `engines.node` selects managed Node 22 on Vercel; local setup documents the same runtime. The ordinary production build and all eight native render traces pass under this runtime, as do 4,377 unit/component/database cases, full lint and post-build TypeScript. Eight optimized-build desktop/mobile login and EN/CS guest-isolation cases pass; the ordinary build still returns 404 for `/dev-preview`. The curl process returns its expected status-22 error for that intentional HTTP 404; the browser suite itself passes. No existing deployment’s runtime patch has been verified or changed in this finding. Upstream evidence: [Happy DOM advisory](https://github.com/capricorn86/happy-dom/security/advisories/GHSA-834v-8h66-3h79), [Undici advisories](https://github.com/nodejs/undici/security/advisories/GHSA-xjj6-f33q-929c), [Node 22.23.4 security release](https://nodejs.org/en/blog/release/v22.23.4), and [Vercel runtime selection and managed patches](https://vercel.com/docs/functions/runtimes/node-js/node-js-versions), checked 24 October 2026. This closes those known advisories, not the remaining application review.

158. Fixed recurring-goal fortnight identity across daylight-saving changes. The old elapsed-millisecond division moved the anchored biweekly window by seven days during Prague/New York summer time, making an already completed goal appear pending again. Calendar-day differences now preserve the existing Monday/1 January 2024 anchor, local midnights and exclusive end. No owner record or recurrence setting changes. Thirty-five added timezone cases cover UTC, Prague, New York, Lord Howe’s half-hour shift and Kathmandu, dates before the anchor, spring/autumn boundaries, completion/reset behavior and every day of a leap year. Eleven of these cases fail against the old implementation; all forty-five new/existing recurrence cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass after the fix. [The installed date-fns API’s calendar-day definition](https://github.com/date-fns/date-fns/blob/v4.1.0/src/differenceInCalendarDays/index.ts) supports the DST-aware calculation. The previous full checkpoint remains 64; the broader Goals UI/release matrix is still pending.

159. Bounded the Music HTTP lifecycle before further pagination/editor work. GET has one eighteen-second budget; POST has one fifty-second budget across authentication, body, limiter, Storage and database work, within the sixty-second platform limit. Authentication also has a ten-second sub-budget and is inside the route's fixed-code error boundary. Expired/denied sessions cannot use a user returned alongside an Auth error; infrastructure failures remain unavailable. The same refusal correction applies to Source settings. Music refuses duplicate/unknown/empty selectors and selected-track/page mixtures before creating an admin client. POST reads at most 8 KiB of strict UTF-8 under the existing five-second whole-body cap, before rate admission or file work; stalled bodies get a retryable 408 and cancellation never waits for a stalled producer hook. Request signals enter separate session/admin transports, and create passes cancellation through file conversion, the native audio probe and persisted confirmation. A late ignored probe result cannot insert after cancellation; a commit whose acknowledgement was cancelled remains intact and an identical retry reconciles it without another upload, probe or insert. All 146 targeted cases across seven route/client/component/native-process/actual-SDK database files, targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. No migration, owner file, provider or production write was involved. The limiter's own Redis transport, complete Music/signing/rotation reads, captured toggle receipts, upload editor/retry lifecycle and unrecorded-object retention remain separate unfinished work. Full checkpoint 64 predates this finding.

160. Music management reads now validate a complete owner page against an exact count, with a 201st sentinel and one batch signing at most the 200 displayed files. A lower REST cap, absent/inconsistent count, malformed/duplicate/out-of-order row or malformed sentinel is unavailable rather than a shorter or empty library. Validation includes explicit owner identity, supported canonical owner/track file paths, source/Instagram identity, safe URLs, non-null tags and database timestamps; ordering preserves PostgreSQL microseconds instead of collapsing them to JavaScript milliseconds. A selected off-page/disabled track is read directly, with exact identity/count confirmation; a successful zero-row read alone confirms absence. Reads/signing share a fifteen-second helper budget within the route's parent budget, with database retries disabled. Signing requires one unique requested path per result and a safe URL bound to that same bucket/object path and a nonempty token. Whole-batch failures or malformed replies are unavailable; an individual unavailable preview retains its management row and the existing disabled “preview unavailable” control. The renderer's object-path check also rejects a different object identity or unsupported extension. No file is deleted or automatically disabled. All 150 targeted cases across seven actual-SDK/complete-migration/client/component/route files, targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. The first diagnostic run exposed an old mock that returned a Promise before the new SDK builder modifiers; the fixture now retains the real chain shape. A malformed signed path also produced a red regression and is now rejected. Those failures are excluded from the final passing evidence. Current [Supabase range](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/range) and [batch signing](https://supabase.com/docs/reference/javascript/storage-from-createsignedurls) contracts were checked. Rotation/renderer read budgets and complete metadata, captured toggle receipts, trend reads, infinite-page reconciliation, upload editor/retry lifecycle and orphan retention remain unfinished. No new migration, production record or provider request was used.


161. Fixed invoice save recovery after the full optimized matrix exposed a mobile retry blocked by feedback/navigation. Failed saves and local validation now use one focused, announced inline message beside the actions, with fixed EN/CS copy and the draft retained. A synchronous submission latch refuses repeated pending writes; existing retry identity and cache reconciliation remain. Desktop keeps the message/actions on one row where they fit. Mobile actions wrap with equal secondary widths and a full-width primary when needed. The shell shares safe-area-aware bottom clearance with native scroll targeting, so validation/focus cannot leave a complete action target behind fixed navigation. A full-hit-area assertion first reproduced the clipping even after a center click passed. Thirty-four invoice/import/database cases, twenty-four related bilingual desktop/mobile browser cases and a final four-case invoice layout recheck pass. Final invoice cases cover six widths, native validation, failure/retry with the same request id, no covering error toast, focus, complete mobile target clearance and axe. Targeted lint/types, the ordinary production build, all eight render traces and post-build types pass. Actual Czech desktop/mobile footer captures were inspected. Earlier development results served stale global CSS and are excluded; the final browser pass used a fresh development cache. The full optimized matrix must still be rerun after the remaining failures are repaired; captured invoice receipt/draft/navigation review and the broader Finance release matrix remain unfinished.

162. Reconciled the seven remaining checkpoint-65 fixture failures with production behavior. Settings classification now exercises owned UUID records, complete reads and the captured-version preference RPC, asserts no direct table write, and keeps sidebar/table movement and failed-write checks. Workflow history enters through Home and asserts canonical `/workflows` before Back/Forward; same-destination navigation remains a no-op. The guide had a real stale-copy issue: EN/CS now reflects the verified 23 October publishing/recovery setup instead of claiming publishing is off and applied migrations still await the owner. It explicitly distinguishes the native first Reel from the still-unverified automatic OwnDashboard Reel flow, preserves consent/allowance and exact-version gates, and points current status to the account/Sources. The dated documentation keeps older evidence labeled historical. Twenty-two targeted browser cases pass with two intentional duplicate skips across current/legacy preference, Workflows and invoice recovery. Twenty-four related component/navigation cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Two initial unit failures were stale hard-coded copy expectations; their corrected final run passes and the diagnostics are excluded. Both Czech marketing guide captures were inspected; six widths, both languages/themes, keyboard links, History, axe, unauthenticated redirect and no marketing request remain covered. A fresh full optimized matrix is still required before release.


163. Fixed Music preview lifecycle and a native browser security block. Starting another picker stops the previous one across mounted instances. Pending and confirmed playback have separate states; a second click cancels startup synchronously. Ten-second startup/playback caps, media-time checks, identity-bound callbacks and unmount cleanup prevent late play promises, old errors or timers from restarting/stopping a newer selection. Stop removes the source and resets the native media element rather than leaving paused buffering; preload is a browser hint, not a hard byte cap. Fixed EN/CS errors expose retry and explicit signed-link refresh; replay waits for the fresh read while existing stop/cancel controls remain usable. A held-refresh native regression reproduced the old-link replay race. More importantly, actual playback revealed that CSP had no `media-src`, so its self-only fallback blocked valid Supabase audio before a request. The policy now narrowly permits self and HTTPS Supabase media. Browser tests assert the actual response directive and native playback instead of overriding the security policy or play method. An isolated `music=live` fixture flag enables Music tests only with the deliberately nonexistent backend; source/mix fixtures and the public tour keep their previous scope. Thirty-nine lifecycle/component/preview-boundary cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Fourteen of fifteen new lifecycle cases fail against the old player. Sixteen EN/CS desktop/mobile native Music, Lab and guest browser cases pass in 49.1 seconds, including six widths, axe, expired-link recovery, a held refresh, real silent-fixture playback, stop/navigation source disposal and zero writes. Czech error-state captures were inspected. Initial runs exposed an absolute read-count fixture assumption, the refresh race and the real CSP block; those diagnostics are excluded. The native Audio recorder was changed to a constructor-preserving Proxy to satisfy TypeScript. Only invented silent bytes were used; no owner file, real provider, spend or publication was involved. Primary [media reset](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLMediaElement/load), [play promise](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/HTMLMediaElement/play) and [CSP media fallback](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/media-src) behavior was checked. Music read reconciliation, selected-track errors, toggle receipts, upload/retry lifecycle, trends and rotation/renderer review remain unfinished; full production validation follows this targeted checkpoint.


164. Weekly calendar time now follows canonical project Own/Freelance classification: a project explicitly marked Own (or a legacy row without engagement) stays own-project work even when linked to an organization. An explicitly named client organization still takes precedence. Three regressions reproduce the old disagreement, including the aggregate split and absence of event text in stored output. Reference legal names, project names and old slugs are normalized once per batch, lazily at its first timed in-range event, instead of again for every block; source order, whole-word/diacritic matching, clipping and summed overlaps remain. Thirty-five library/component cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. The reproducible [local CPU benchmark](weekly-channels-benchmark.mjs) checks exact aggregate parity on 1,000 invented events, 100 projects and 25 organizations before alternating 21 samples. Median time falls from 43.60 ms to 4.42 ms (89.85%) on Node 22.23.4; [raw samples](weekly-channels-cpu.json) record the source hash and scope. This measures synthetic local CPU, not production latency, database work or financial savings. Its parity fixture excludes the intentionally corrected classification case, which has separate tests. No UI layout, stored review or provider was changed. The weekly editor's captured writes, owner/week rollover, complete focus read and remaining aggregate-input checks remain unfinished; checkpoint 66 predates this finding.


165. Fixed shared install/toolbar controls on 25 October. A denied install-dismissal storage read no longer throws from the shell effect; dismissal still lasts for the current document when storage writes fail. Current installed/dismissed refs supersede captured mount booleans. The browser invitation is consumed synchronously before awaiting its single-use native prompt, duplicate actions/replacement invitations are refused while it is pending, and completion after unmount makes no UI update. Install, later and close actions share 44 px mobile / 32 px desktop targets. Toolbar search/back use the same target heights, including the 640–767 px mobile interval; the single title and workspace-back behavior remain. Nine lifecycle cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile install/shared-toggle browser cases pass in 35.9 seconds, including eight widths, native keyboard activation, blocked storage, dismissal after another invitation, target bounds, mobile-navigation clearance, axe and no console errors. Czech banner crops were inspected. The initial storage denial is a reproduced product failure; other diagnostic failures came from an unpinned test language and Happy DOM binding a throwing prototype spy onto a reused storage instance. Final cases use explicit dictionary state and a fresh actual Storage shim per trial. Those diagnostics are excluded. [The single-use gesture requirement](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/BeforeInstallPromptEvent/prompt) was checked against the current browser reference. Browser invitations were synthetic fixtures; no real installation, login, provider or owner record changed. Checkpoint 66 predates this targeted fix.


166. Weekly focus now reads one complete, owned Monday-to-Monday snapshot instead of every historical generation through a capped nested REST read. Migration 114 selects the latest generation per day before returning at most seven sets / 49 ordered items, refuses oversized legacy text and JSON, and preserves invoker RLS, a fixed search path and authenticated-only execution. No index, table, provider or stored row changes. The caller validates owner/week/set/item identities, date/position order, duplicates and text/response bounds, uses one fifteen-second cancellable RPC and disables automatic retries. Initial failure no longer invents an empty recap or 0/0; failed cached results retain a stale warning. Explicit retry preserves the typed draft. Carry-forward navigation and completion wait for confirmed history, while Save Draft remains available. Public tour behavior remains read-only and shows no fabricated recap. Home's best-completed-generation garden is unchanged.

Ninety-one targeted cases across seven files pass, including seven full-chain database cases, 1,200 old generations followed by the actual latest set, all 49 tasks, foreign owners, week edges, empty latest sets, input/response refusal, grants, real SDK failures/cancellation and ten EN/CS mounted recovery cases. Read-schema generation from all 114 migrations, targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. Four EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass with explicit retry, no writes, six widths, long unbroken title/project names, whole-card and intrinsic content bounds, axe and no unexpected console errors. Czech desktop/mobile captures were inspected. The first browser attempts used an incorrect semantic-title locator and an alert assertion excluding its Retry text; those diagnostics are excluded. Screenshot inspection then exposed a real implicit-grid column widening to about 2,077 CSS pixels at a 360 px viewport despite document-overflow checks passing. A new whole-card bound assertion reproduced it before explicit base grid columns/minimum sizing fixed it. The final checks measure content/card bounds in the same layout snapshot and wait for resize transitions. All 28 audit migrations remain prepared/unapplied. Checkpoint 66 predates this finding; the editor's captured saves, owner/week rollover and exit guards remain unfinished.


167. Prepared the captured weekly-review database/client contract before replacing the legacy editor. Migration 115 binds actor, record id, Monday and exact source version, serializes absent-row creation, preserves unknown legacy item keys, returns explicit conflicts and acknowledges identical retries without another write. Completion is retained on automatic navigation; explicit Save Draft reopens it. Input/receipt bounds, requested-field validation, strict aggregate-only Calendar input, full persisted-row verification and authenticated-only invoker execution are covered. No table/index or stored-row rewrite. The client captures inputs before its first await, validates owner/week/values/versions and retained fields, disables SDK retries, supports a fifteen-second caller-cancellable budget and uses fixed private error messages. Receipt application cannot resurrect a missing source or replace a newer cached edit. Forty-one cases pass across twelve actual full-chain database cases and twenty-nine client/runtime/cache cases; targeted lint, TypeScript and read-schema generation from 115 files pass. Initial direct database fixture reads discarded microseconds; final fixtures use PostgreSQL JSON serialization. Explicit parentheses corrected nested JSON subtraction, with a valid fractional-aggregate test added. Those diagnostic failures are excluded. Twenty-nine migrations remain prepared/unapplied. Editor integration, owner/week lifecycle and guarded exits remain unfinished; checkpoint 67 predates this contract.


168. Replaced weekly planning's direct upsert with finding 167's captured owner/week/version save. Inputs and edited fields are captured before waiting; Next advances only after the persisted receipt. Stable creation/objective identities survive a failed retry, and a typed objective is included in Save Draft even before Add. Unrequested summary/legacy lists are omitted from the patch. The editor reserves synchronously, disables fields while pending and registers immediate dirty/pending protection with the shared navigation guard. Refused saves retain the entire draft with fixed inline EN/CS recovery. Conflicts leave text read-only/selectable, block another save and reload the exact owner/week only after explicit discard. Late callbacks are cancelled on unmount or context change. Account/Monday rollover freezes the captured draft; explicit discard uses a full server reload so Calendar and owner data cannot retain the old context. Parent refreshes cannot adopt a newer source version underneath dirty text. Confirmed cache application preserves later records and avoids an extra collection read when successful; ambiguity/conflicts invalidate only the exact review key, avoiding an unrelated focus read. Only aggregate Calendar minutes/counts are written.

Four mounted regressions reproduce early advancement, writable pending input, account reuse and rollover against the old editor. The final 142-case, nine-file targeted run passes, including twelve mounted save/lifecycle cases, four exact-reload SDK cases, existing focus recovery/ownership and aggregate checks. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass in 29.1 seconds with held 503s, disabled fields, refused duplicate actions, pending sidebar exit/discard protection, retained retry payloads, no extra confirmed-save collection read, selectable conflict drafts, explicit reload, six widths, whole-card/content bounds, axe and no direct table writes. Czech conflict captures were inspected. Diagnostic assumptions about a disabled fieldset's child `.disabled` property and a portal outside the test container were corrected. A later native attempt queried background accessible roles while the modal correctly hid them; final checks close Stay before examining the underlying alert. Lazy UUID refs use React's supported null-initialization pattern after lint rejected an empty-string sentinel. These diagnostics are excluded. The duplicate step title was removed from the progress label; remaining summary-label/action-row cleanup and aggregate-input review stay open. All 29 migrations remain prepared/unapplied; checkpoint 67 predates this editor change.


169. Cleaned the weekly summary/action layout after screenshot review. The summary has one visible title while its textarea retains an accessible label. Back/Next have matching widths; Save Draft/Complete share equal-width, equal-height grid cells on one row, with 44 px mobile / 32 px desktop minimum targets. Shorter Czech draft wording keeps both actions on one line at 360 px; longer labels can still wrap without clipping. The retained-draft warning uses shorter EN/CS copy. A native size/row assertion fails against the old footer before this change. Twenty-five mounted weekly cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Twelve EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass in 29.9 seconds, including six-width whole-card and matching-action bounds, minimum heights, real keyboard selection of read-only conflict text, guarded recovery and axe. Final Czech desktop/mobile captures were inspected and stored with the report. No save semantics, provider, publication or owner data changed. Remaining aggregate/legacy-input review and final release stay open.


170. Fixed Career cover-letter placeholder lookup. An unknown `{{constructor}}` previously reached inherited object members and threw `value.trim is not a function`; inherited known variables could also replace text the caller never supplied. Two regressions reproduce both outcomes. The helper now recognizes only its own four placeholder keys and the caller's own supplied variables, retaining unknown placeholders literally and existing neutral fallbacks. No saved letter, evidence suggestion, model or provider changes. Twenty-five template and existing Career workflow cases pass across two files. Targeted lint passes. Remaining Career editor/provider review is separate.


171. Replaced translucent focus colors with the opaque semantic information color in both themes, and added a system `Highlight` outline under forced colors. Native before-fix checks reproduce about 1.42:1 button focus contrast on the light canvas and no outline when shadows disappear. CSS analysis separately gives about 1.45:1 on white and 2.39:1 on the dark surface. These fail the current [W3C non-text contrast guidance](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/non-text-contrast.html) for authored focus states; [Focus Visible](https://www.w3.org/WAI/WCAG22/Understanding/focus-visible.html) explains the same relationship. Eighty measured button, input, textarea, select and checkbox states now range from 5.51:1 to 8.75:1 across EN/CS, both themes, desktop/mobile and six button widths. [Raw native color samples and source hashes](focus-contrast.json) distinguish before/after scope; the canvas resolves colors to 8-bit precision and ratio calculations remain unrounded. Forced-color button/field/select checks preserve a solid outline at least 2 px wide.

Twenty-six native browser cases pass in 57.6 seconds, including all ten focus cases, eight existing toggle/target cases and eight guarded weekly-save cases. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass; all four light/dark Czech dialog captures and the refreshed weekly conflict captures were inspected. The first measurement caught an in-progress field style transition; final checks wait for the computed ring/outline rather than reading its first frame. That diagnostic is separate from the reproduced contrast and forced-color failures. No geometry, owner data, provider or save semantics changed. Screenshot inspection also found black date/time picker glyphs in the dark app; selected native color-scheme handling remains the next repair. Full optimized release verification follows this change; checkpoint 68 predates it.


172. Declared the selected native color scheme: light at the root, dark under the existing app class, and light for print. The prior dark Calendar captures showed black date/time picker glyphs; a native regression confirms the root reported `normal` even when the app selected dark. Browser controls now follow the app choice with the OS preference deliberately set to the opposite theme. This follows the [browser vendor's color-scheme guidance](https://web.dev/articles/color-scheme); no theme preference, field value, provider or stored record changes. The updated dark desktop/mobile captures show visible white picker glyphs.

Thirty EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass in 54.9 seconds, including all twelve focus/native-theme/forced-color/print cases, eight existing invoice PDF-import/recovery/document-reflow/print-isolation cases and ten Calendar/Date/Goal checks. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. An initial post-change run failed because the served development stylesheet retained the previous focus fix but omitted all three new color-scheme declarations. Fetching the CSS actually linked by the page confirmed that mismatch. Only the verified task server was stopped; its cache was preserved, and the fresh server's CSS contains all three declarations. That stale-asset run is excluded. Ordinary optimized verification follows; checkpoint 68 predates these shared-style changes.


173. Fixed DNESKAi field feedback and diagnostic recovery. Caption counts now include whitespace and the keyword line the server restores, using the existing UTF-16 limit; DM/public replies count their trimmed saved text in the same units. Alt text has its existing 1,000-unit limit in both counter and draft validation, avoiding invalid preview requests. Ordinary slide text retains trimmed NFC code-point counting. No authored text, server limit, approval hash, provider or stored record is changed by these checks.

Unknown diagnostic fields now use own translation keys, so a schema-valid `constructor` cannot become an inherited function label. Lists over twenty problems offer all remaining entries and retain blocking-first order. A long DM preview is a named keyboard-scrollable region. Lab shortcuts respect handled events and focused native controls/regions, allowing arrow scrolling while preserving root-level queue/slide shortcuts.

Eight initial regressions reproduce the original counter, inherited-label and hidden-remainder failures. Sixty-three component/domain cases pass across six files. Sixteen EN/CS desktop/mobile native cases pass in 58.7 seconds with code frozen, covering counters including emoji/combining marks, restored keyword length, alt text, actual keyboard scrolling, axe/reflow, existing save/approval and Lab dirty/navigation/shortcut flows. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass; Czech desktop/mobile Lab captures were inspected. Earlier development runs exposed the inaccessible preview and intercepted arrows; both are repaired. One existing dirty-state case failed during a source-changing development run and passes in the frozen-input repeat; optimized release verification remains required. The ledger has 644 entries (198 pending, 86 partial); remaining editor/provider coverage is separate.


174. Bounded the shared Marketing input reader by both bytes and a five-second read deadline. It preserves valid signed UTF-8 text, rejects malformed/excessive declared lengths, combines request/caller cancellation, normalizes private stream failures, cancels stalled/oversized input without waiting for an uncooperative cleanup hook, and always releases its reader/timer. Ten regressions fail on the old implementation. Twenty-three body/knowledge API cases now pass across two files, including real stream lock/cancel behavior, multibyte bounds, malformed UTF-8, pre-cancellation and a stalled stream under a controlled clock. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. No database/provider operation is added; caller authentication, response classification and persistence review remain separate. The source ledger has 644 entries (197 pending, 86 partial).


175. Publication recovery no longer invents a successful `queued` result when the database receipt is null, missing or malformed. Known refusal codes require own mapping keys; unknown messages, thrown transports and late cancelled results cannot become acknowledgements or leak their text. The owner route rejects auth errors with cached users, reads at most 4 KiB through the shared bounded reader, propagates request cancellation through a fifteen-second operation budget and turns off SDK retries. It does not call Meta, alter approval, change a ceiling or change the existing SQL recovery rules. An old source comment no longer labels the deployed recovery migration unapplied.

Twelve initial regressions fail on the old route. Thirty-eight receipt/body cases pass across two files, including a late uncooperative RPC and body timeout before any write. Two native desktop/mobile API-boundary cases pass in 1.1 seconds, now including publication recovery. Their Origin follows the configured preview URL; the previous hardcoded-port development run correctly returned CSRF refusals and is excluded. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. The source ledger has 644 entries (195 pending, 86 partial); final optimized verification and remaining source coverage are unfinished.


176. Prepared the captured Tasks write foundation. A stable default adds a finite task version without rewriting legacy row contents; all future updates advance it monotonically. Create/toggle/delete bind owner, id and source identity under invoker RLS. Only requested completion/version changes may persist, stale and ABA changes conflict, absence is distinct from deletion, and suppressed/altered or post-trigger writes roll back. One private metadata-only create receipt prevents an ambiguous retry from recreating a deliberately removed task. It retains no second task-text copy, is owner-only and immutable to session update/delete, and cascades with the owner account. Creates share the repository import lock and collection ceiling. Existing Task text, GLOBAL rules, associations and repository fields remain intact.

Sixty-seven targeted contract/database/repository cases pass across four files, including 22 actual migrated-database cases and real SQL-to-client receipt validation. The SDK boundary captures before awaiting, confirms field/owner/id/version, uses one bounded non-retrying call and patches only its captured cache row. Exact reload distinguishes confirmed absence from failure. Targeted lint/TypeScript and offline nine-table read generation from 116 migrations pass. The migration guide records limits, permissions, retention and rollback boundaries. A fixture's missing project slug was corrected before passing checks. No production record was changed. Thirty migrations (87–116) remain prepared/unapplied; CLI authentication still blocks rollout. Tasks UI adoption, native recovery and density work remain unfinished. The source ledger has 645 entries (158 pending, 114 partial).


177. Adopted captured Task creation in both the full form and compact widget. Form state is isolated from the grouped list. One creation id survives uncertain responses and closed/reopened drafts; success clears only its captured draft. Same-frame duplicate submits and exits are blocked while pending. Owner change/unmount aborts and suppresses late callbacks; changed or removed receipts retain selectable text until explicit discard. GLOBAL, related-record and time bounds remain intact. Unicode title length now matches PostgreSQL characters without silently truncating input.

A real React Query regression reproduced an older recovery read hiding a confirmed creation. Successful writes now cancel that exact collection read before applying the verified row, avoiding another collection request on ordinary success. Seventy-nine targeted cases across six files pass, including eleven mounted recovery cases and 23 migrated-database cases. Eight EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass in 20.1 seconds, covering pending dismissal, identical retries, conflict selection, six widths, equal action sizes, axe and console privacy. Czech desktop/mobile captures were inspected. The native harness is isolated to the explicit fake backend; the original preview identity correctly refused a real UUID-bound write before that harness was added. The test excludes only the intentional fake endpoint's 503 browser diagnostic; private response text is never displayed. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Toggle/delete and repository cleanup/density adoption remain separate. The source ledger has 646 entries (158 pending, 114 partial); thirty migrations remain prepared/unapplied.


178. Replaced legacy Task completion/deletion acknowledgements and whole-list optimistic rollback with the checked owner/version contract. Each row is reserved synchronously before a confirmation can await; unrelated rows remain usable. Deletion captures the complete original source before showing confirmation. No local completion/removal is shown until the persisted receipt arrives. Cache application cancels older exact reads and modifies only its captured version; newer rows and independent successes remain intact. Conflict, confirmed absence and unavailable writes have separate localized handling. Same-frame navigation is guarded; account change/unmount aborts and suppresses late writes/callbacks.

Thirty-four mounted Task creation/action/cleanup/refresh cases pass across four files, including thirteen new action cases and a held real QueryClient read. All sixteen combined EN/CS desktop/mobile creation/action browser cases pass in 35.2 seconds, covering duplicate control activation, pending-state truth, independent completion during failure, conflict reload, cancelled deletion and confirmed removal of one row. Only the intentional fake endpoint's 503 diagnostic is excluded; private error text is absent. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Repository-source cleanup, list density, broader source review and final release remain unfinished. The ledger has 647 entries (158 pending, 114 partial). No owner record/provider write was used for verification; thirty migrations remain prepared/unapplied.


179. Preserved permanent repository identity through Task source reads and cleanup commits. Batch reads confirm the numeric repository id, branch and both candidate file entries in one immutable commit; file contents are pinned to that head and checked against the expected blob and byte count. Missing/unavailable metadata, a reused name, mismatched contents or a failed pinned read retains tasks. Only confirmed absence of both paths in the identified commit is missing-source evidence. The existing four-repository concurrency/result budgets remain. Cleanup clones its task sources before awaiting, rejects missing/mixed/noncanonical repository ids, and removes the unverified fallback read.

The final commit boundary now receives the permanent repository id. Reviewed edits use GitHub’s permanent branch node plus an expected head, compare the reviewed blob and confirm repository/ref/head/parent/path/blob/size in the receipt. Identical content creates no additional commit. Other existing replacement/edit callers preserve their previous contract. Commit/Needed authentication also rejects an error with a cached user. The implementation follows GitHub’s [branch-node and expected-head commit contract](https://docs.github.com/en/graphql/reference/commits) and [Git entry fields](https://docs.github.com/en/graphql/reference/git). Three actual query documents/variable payloads validate offline against [Octokit’s published GitHub schema](https://github.com/octokit/graphql-schema/blob/master/schema.graphql). That downloaded schema initially failed SDL validation because two unrelated EnterpriseOwnerInfo fields were duplicated; only those identical signatures were deduplicated before the strict validation passed. No live authenticated provider query or commit was used.

All 133 targeted client/server/source/cleanup cases pass across eight files, including nineteen new permanent-branch contract cases, route-level write receipts and the mounted identity-forwarding regression. TypeScript and targeted lint pass after correcting the new test mock’s optional RequestInit typing; the earlier type diagnostic is excluded. The source ledger has 648 entries (158 pending, 114 partial). Repository Refresh/cleanup owner/pending lifecycle, list density and broader release work remain open. Thirty migrations are still prepared/unapplied.


180. Replaced nested Task subcards with rows and shared dividers. Short titles and related metadata share a desktop row when they fit; rich/mobile metadata wraps without displacing actions. One open group and its completed list use matching centered bounded widths. Unequal content keeps its natural height. Completion/delete/reopen targets are consistently visible at 44 px mobile/32 px desktop. Group/finished/limit controls expose their expanded state and controlled list. The default-five limit and shared thirty-second clock remain. Row layout animation is removed. Measured three-line titles now offer a keyboard-accessible, selectable full-text disclosure, with observer cleanup on unmount. Confirmed row removal restores lost focus to an available neighbor or Add, while preserving focus that moved elsewhere and suppressing stale owner/unmount callbacks.

Thirty-five mounted Task cases pass across four files; targeted lint and TypeScript pass. All 24 EN/CS desktop/mobile Task creation/action/density/project-identity cases pass in 1.0 minute. Native evidence includes six widths, both themes, long text expansion, rich metadata, equal/centered widths, visible action dimensions, group limits, focus restoration, action center/edge hit checks above navigation, axe and console checks. Czech desktop/mobile captures were inspected. Diagnostic tests were corrected for a changing Show-all label, importance sorting and nested locator scope. An interrupted nested-locator run is excluded. Rounded button corners correctly fall outside the hit shape; checks now sample the center and four edge midpoints, following the measured-target rule instead of changing valid UI. No owner/provider write was made. The refreshed HTML plan has eighteen authentic desktop/mobile pairs and ten coverage rows; both modes, all images, controls, six-width reflow, axe and page errors pass. 181. Completed the shared bilingual diagnostic for the alt-text limit introduced in finding 173. Field-level guidance already existed; the common code dictionary now explains the same 1,000-character refusal and supplies the established edit recovery action. The existing complete emitted-code scan reproduced the missing entry; all 24 code/field-feedback cases now pass across two files, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. No additional test mirrors the dictionary entry. Complete dictionary reconciliation remains separate.
182. Added bilingual, field-linked guidance for invalid task time estimates. Whole minutes must be 1–100,000 or empty; incomplete native numeric input (such as `e`) also stays invalid rather than becoming an empty saved estimate. The draft remains editable and saving stays unavailable until correction. Native tests caught React's unchanged-empty-value `onChange` gap; the input event now captures that browser validity state too. Updated the older fixture to assert actual disabled/accessible feedback and zero `write_task` requests, and bound the confirmation-centering fixture to complete owned UUID/version rows without weakening production validation. The final targeted matrix passes 13 EN/CS desktop/mobile cases with one intentional desktop-only variant skipped in 29.5 seconds; 24 mounted action/composer cases, targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Full optimized release validation remains pending after this repair.
183. Removed quadratic rescanning from public feed text cleanup. A run of opening angle brackets without a closing tag previously retried the tag expression at every position before truncation; the replacement matches that tail once and retains its literal text. Complete-tag cleanup, Unicode/control handling, output truncation and instruction signals are unchanged. On Node 22.23.4, five local CPU samples have medians of 503.865 ms before and 0.0668 ms after for 32,768 unmatched brackets; balanced 92,800-byte and plain 90,000-byte samples also show no regression. Exact JSON parity holds for 5,000 seeded inputs and six limits. All 32 content/native-feed cases pass across two files, including an actual source projection whose instruction signals occur beyond the visible summary. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. [Raw samples](feed-sanitizer-cpu.json) measure local synthetic CPU, not production latency or billed savings.
184. Stopped shared bounded text reads from waiting on an untrusted stream producer's cancellation hook after detecting oversize. A stalled hook previously held the result forever; a rejected hook replaced the truthful truncated result with the producer's error. Cancellation now starts once, is observed without awaiting it and releases the reader lock; structured callers still refuse truncated JSON. Both failures reproduce against the former helper and pass after the fix. All 95 bounded-reader/GitHub-file/Calendar/repository-batch/cron/stack/GoCardless cases pass across eight files, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. Existing abort behavior, character bounds and split UTF-8 decoding remain covered. No actual provider request was used.
185. Completed the Tasks repository action lifecycle. Refresh and cleanup now share a synchronous operation reservation and exit guard; owner identity is captured before the first await, and cleanup clones exact source rows before confirmation. Account change/unmount cancels reads, source work and database consumption; late callbacks cannot change notices or a later account's GitHub cache. Scans and SQL helpers carry caller cancellation and capture input arrays before waiting. Changed tasks are rechecked before external source commits, and only captured rows confirmed by the source step reach conditional SQL consumption. Missing/failed files, failed commits, reopened/conflicting records and incomplete repository lists retain their existing conservative behavior. The pending cleanup control stays mounted and disabled; a disconnected result no longer also announces nothing-to-clear. All 66 mounted/helper/cache cases pass across five files, with clean targeted lint and TypeScript. The combined native Tasks matrix passes all 32 EN/CS desktop/mobile cases in 1.4 minutes, including six widths, axe, failed commit, conditional-consumption conflict, no duplicate source commit on retry and pending navigation. The initial new browser fixture omitted its owned repository project and was corrected; that failed/interrupted diagnostic is excluded from passing evidence. No actual GitHub source commit or owner row was changed.
186. Corrected picture availability across deck swaps and insertion. The server resolves kept slides first, then checks insertions against their final pictures; source eligibility still comes from the saved deck and primary source. The browser uses the same current-draft availability, allowing removed or replaced pictures to return without admitting a new supporting source. Four regression cases fail before the repair and pass after it, including both swap/insertion orders and retained supporting-source eligibility. All 32 deck/revision cases pass across two files, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. Six native desktop/mobile cases pass in EN/CS for swapped-picture reuse and the existing type/insertion/save/approval flow; no owner API, actual storage or publication is used. Final app-wide release remains separate.
187. Made numeric character references safe in source-picture metadata and Career feed text. Both existing decoders share a Unicode scalar guard: out-of-range, surrogate and non-finite references stay literal; valid decimal/hex characters and each decoder's existing replacement order remain unchanged. Ten new integration cases fail before the repair and pass after it. They inspect actual locally encoded JPEGs carrying malformed XMP credits and parse multi-item job feeds without discarding unaffected entries. All 40 picture/feed cases pass across two files, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. No network, paid media, source attribution change or owner-data mutation is used.
188. Preserved query-based vacancy identity in Career. Unknown postings use the existing URL resource key, retaining job-id query parameters and ports while ignoring UTM tracking, fragments and parameter order. Verified dated aliases keep their existing first-match evidence. An immutable alias lookup replaces repeated full-array URL parsing. The query collision fails before the repair; all ten final helper/mounted cases pass, including a real Jobs panel whose unrelated saved/applied siblings no longer disable saving the third vacancy. The mounted baseline also reproduces the wrong saved state after its missing Tooltip provider/settling fixture is corrected. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass; two existing desktop/mobile research/dialog/keyboard/axe cases pass in 8.2 seconds. Five interleaved Node 22.23.4 CPU samples have a 15.204 ms before median and 0.549 ms after median for 1,110 lookups, with exact research-result parity. [Raw samples](career-alias-cpu.json) measure local synthetic CPU, not production latency or billing. No live vacancy, application or saved owner row changes.
189. Stopped treating media storage outages as cold caches. Slide and Reel lookups permit rendering only for typed object-not-found responses, including the legacy SDK's 404 code. Permission, missing bucket/tenant, unknown service errors and empty successful responses fail before signing or uploading. Twelve boundary cases fail before the repair; all 55 cache/Reel/worker cases pass across three files afterward, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. A composed Reel/storage-adapter regression proves a service outage loads no slides and starts no encode or upload. The local absent-object fixture now carries the SDK's typed status instead of an unstructured message. The distinction follows the installed SDK error normalization and [Supabase's storage error contract](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/storage/debugging/error-codes); generic legacy 404 remains the SDK's compatibility case. No bucket, object, credential or production record changed.
190. Reused physical Reel media across nonvisual revision edits. The version-two key covers ordered approved JPEG hashes, exact audio bytes/id and encoding policy; caption, schedule and alt text remain in each revision's approval hash. Shared storage metadata no longer names a single revision. Before cache access the reader verifies the full stored revision hash, clean report, complete JPEG set and valid render keys; before encoding it checks loaded JPEG bytes against the revision. Four regressions fail before the repair. All 45 Reel/render/format/publish-due cases pass across four files, including both actual local ffmpeg encodes, caption/time/alt reuse with only one encode, changed track bytes, tampered slides and legacy record lookup restricted to the exact revision hash. Targeted lint and TypeScript pass. Fixtures now use actual synthetic JPEG-byte hashes instead of unrelated hash constants. Encoding settings, approval/publication decisions and production objects are unchanged; concurrent admission and native work bounds remain separate leads.
191. Reused immutable Prague date/time formatters in planning, Calendar and the Lab. Date resolution and local-input formatting share one fixed formatter, and the Lab uses the workspace's shared time helper. No locale, timezone, output or date-validation rule changes. All 60 planning/workspace/Lab/deck cases pass across four files, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. A local comparison confirms exact calendar/queue JSON parity for sixty synthetic posts, 194 instant checks around both 2026 DST transitions, eight wall-clock conversions and both ambiguous/missing-time refusals. Five interleaved Node 22.23.4 samples have medians of 119.191 ms before and 5.996 ms after for three calendar/queue projections. [Raw samples](prague-formatting-cpu.json) measure local CPU, not production latency or bills. Two native desktop/mobile Lab-approval/Calendar/Posts cases pass with the browser timezone set to America/Los_Angeles in 6.7 seconds; no owner API is used.
192. Restored legacy Storage object-miss compatibility without relaxing outage handling. The installed SDK can report HTTP 400 with body statusCode 404; that pair now joins the existing typed object-absence cases. Two focused adapter regressions fail before the repair. All 55 slide-cache/Reel/migrated dry-day integration cases pass across three files afterward in 16.80 seconds, with targeted lint and TypeScript passing. The original emulator and actual SDK are unchanged: the complete fake-provider night, generation, render, approval/edit, publication/reconciliation and visual artifacts work again. No real provider call, paid request, publication or storage change occurs. This corrects the application regression found by checkpoint 74; it does not convert that failed checkpoint into a pass.
193. Enforced positional public slide rules across DNESKAi. Prompt `marketing-copy/8`, generation validation/deck assembly, editor fields, fit, render policy `dneskai-render/3`, readiness, Lab holds and Reel reuse now share headline-only first-slide checks. Public sources slides, credits and other unplaced text are held. Four initial regressions fail before the repair. Legacy text remains stored and visible through an explicit draft-cleanup action; removing it saves a successor that needs new rendering and approval. Source removal never restores forbidden content to satisfy the two-slide minimum. Approved, unclaimed posts can be returned with a note through the existing server decision; claimed/published/ambiguous states remain protected. The preview now mirrors queued-publication cancellation. Prepared migration 117 repeats the structural hold inside the locked owner approval and filters due claims before their limit, without rewriting any row. Twelve isolated SQL cases cover legacy refusal, intact records, Unicode whitespace, permissions and valid/blocked claims. All 222 final generation/render/editor/SQL/migrated-day/mounted cases pass across eleven files in 17.42 seconds; full ESLint and TypeScript pass. Read types regenerate from all 117 migrations. Ten EN/CS desktop/mobile browser cases pass in 1.1 minutes, with four final capture rechecks in 29.6 seconds; the new cases include axe, overflow, protected return, cleanup, real preview and new-version save, without owner API calls or console errors. Inspected Czech control crops join the visual plan. Older renderer fixtures now distinguish public types from refused legacy sources, and the picture-less fake model keeps its body on a later slide. Earlier fixture and narrow-type diagnostics are excluded. Thirty-one migrations remain prepared/unapplied; no actual model, provider, publication, reply, credential or owner-row action was performed. Model-written CTA enforcement and other source leads remain separate.

## Database evidence

Read-only production advisors were inspected for the `own-dashboard` EU project. Findings need catalog verification before changes: 73 RLS init-plan warnings, 70 unindexed foreign keys, 36 unused indexes, one duplicate-index warning, 15 executable security-definer function warnings and nine intentionally private tables without browser policies. Advisor warnings alone do not justify extra indexes, index deletion or wider grants. No secret rows or credential values were read.

Prepared fixes: `20261023164118_app_wide_rls_initplans.sql` converts the 73 direct identity calls to statement InitPlans, preserving every policy role and predicate. `20261023170010_project_parent_rls_lookup.sql` resolves project-write policy expansion in local PostgreSQL 18.6 with a security-invoker lookup that retains RLS. The production PostgreSQL 17 read-only planner check accepted the existing update. Ten tests pass across the real upgrade and complete engagement-aggregate suites; ownership, related parents, grants and anonymous access are covered. The current local generated read schema was rebuilt from all 116 migrations. Neither new migration has been applied remotely yet.

Pending source and SQL changes were preserved and dependencies reinstalled. Subsequent release checks run in `/Users/lukasbarsinbars/own-dashboard-audit-work-20261023`; results from interrupted commands are not counted as passing.

## Finding 204 — bind Career expiry to the requested job

Any expired `validThrough` in arbitrary scripts could mark the requested position closed, including another job's recommendation. The generic presence of any JobPosting type could also establish an open state. JSON-LD evidence now requires a JobPosting with the exact normalized title and registered URL, including query identity. Arrays and `@graph` are traversed with bounded nodes/scripts; conflicting or malformed matching dates stay unknown. A redirected different job cannot inherit the original listing's identity. Challenges are checked before expiry; mixed apply/closed copy is unknown, and script-only application phrases are ignored. Explicit 404/410 handling stays intact.

Primary research on 25 October confirms [JobPosting](https://schema.org/JobPosting) describes one job and its Date/DateTime expiry; [Google's guide](https://developers.google.com/search/docs/appearance/structured-data/job-posting) relates expiry to that posting. Exact URL/title matching and conservative date handling are local safeguards, not claims that the providers require this algorithm. Date-only values wait until the date ends in UTC−12; offset-free datetimes and invalid calendar dates cannot supply a precise closure.

Nine new cases fail before the repair. All 45 availability/Career workflow cases pass across two files in 384 ms, with targeted ESLint and TypeScript passing. Tests cover unrelated types/titles/URLs, matching graphs, conflicts, query-based redirects, challenge/mixed copy, script-only phrases, date-only/invalid dates and incomplete graph traversal, using synthetic fetch only. Seventeen existing native Career cases pass with three intentional duplicate skips in 59.6 seconds: import recovery, preserved application drafts/history, stage transitions, saved account timezone, prepared queue and Oslo/Czech archive controls with six-width and keyboard checks. These are targeted development checks, not the final optimized release matrix. Whole-page HTML CPU and the broader complete source/route reads remain separate leads; no live vacancy or stored owner record changes.

This is reproduced evidence, not an implemented repair or a production timing/billing claim.


## Finding 205 — bounded Career page parsing

The reproduced malformed HTML takes more than the worker's 500 ms wall cutoff on only 100 KB of unterminated tags or 175 KB of unterminated scripts. Whole-page backtracking expressions are replaced with one bounded scan that consumes quoted tags, comments, script/style blocks and actual attributes. It preserves Unicode offsets with ASCII-only case folding and ignores commented closure/metadata, data-type lookalikes and quoted values impersonating type. The first duplicate type attribute wins; unquoted JSON-LD MIME values are supported. Visible-text title matching now lowercases the page once.

The classifier refuses incomplete markup and documents beyond 1.5 million characters, 10,000 tags, 4,096 characters per tag or forty structured scripts; database/provider reads remain unchanged. Its existing 1.5 MB download cap and five-hundred-node JSON graph bound also remain. A refused or partial page is unknown, never a closure based on the available prefix. This is a deliberately bounded availability scanner, not a general browser HTML parser.

All 58 availability/Career workflow cases pass across two files in 357 ms; targeted ESLint and TypeScript pass. Cases include the two pathological inputs, huge/unfinished markup, comments, quoted delimiters, mixed case, Unicode expansion, attribute impersonation/duplicates, excess scripts and the earlier identity/cancellation matrix. The same offline worker probe now completes the malformed inputs as unknown in 3.963416 ms and 0.324417 ms of local execution; the valid plain control remains open at 0.584 ms. `career-html-cpu.json` records baseline cutoffs, one-sample timings, source hash and method. These are local execution observations, not production performance or billed savings. No real provider or owner record is touched.

## Finding 210 — invalid payment details still produced invoice QR codes

Twenty-four initial regressions reproduce unchecked account/checksum, non-finite/unpayable/over-format amounts, field injection, impossible dates and malformed currency acceptance. A present invalid explicit IBAN cannot silently fall back to a different domestic account. Foreign accounts retain generic structure/checksum support; this is not a full country/bank registry or account-ownership check.

Amounts must remain positive after two-decimal formatting and within 9,999,999.99. Currency uses the runtime canonical list, retaining recognized values outside static FX; unknown and injected codes are refused. A due date must be a real date in its exact calendar form, independent of local timezone. Optional symbol/message fields are bounded and type-checked before their existing formatting. Existing valid SPAYD output, message cleanup, arithmetic, static FX and recorded data remain unchanged. The missing-QR explanation now names the invoice's actual payment details instead of suggesting that changing global settings rewrites an existing snapshot.

Fourteen mounted real-detail EN/CS cases verify valid output, retained readable malformed-field documents and zero-total refusal, without fixture mutation. After correcting an initially incomplete language mock, two actual invalid-date cases still fail because display formatting throws; unreadable date strings now display a dash instead of crashing the document. All 131 targeted invoice, parser/import, settlement and component cases pass across six files in 2.01 seconds; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass.

A native macOS Vision decoder recovers the exact complete synthetic payment payload from all twelve actual dev-preview QR captures across EN/CS and 360, 430, 768, 1024, 1440 and 1728px, with no page errors or document overflow. The separate native public-resource and one-page Czech A4/dark-print checks pass (two cases, 5.7 seconds). The PDF barcode itself is not independently decoded. ## Finding 211 — project traffic ownership, completeness and recovery

Eight initial regressions reproduce analytics outages as successful zeros, mismatched permanent repository fallback, malformed responses, unbound deployment ownership and missing repeat-read reuse. The API now confirms authenticated identity, the captured owner header and owned project metadata before using the deployment credential. Auth errors cannot reuse a cached user. The existing DASHBOARD_OWNER_ID binds that credential to its owner; a missing/mismatched configuration fails closed. Only four project columns are read with explicit owner/repository filters and disabled SDK retries. Several owned sections may share a repository, so this bounded lookup proves existence rather than claiming a complete project list. No RLS or schema changes.

Filtered provider lookup refuses ambiguity, continued pages and wrong links; permanent repository id never falls back to a short project name. Legacy matching requires the full linked repository name. The explicit inclusive UTC thirty-day window includes a separate period count and a daily aggregate requesting thirty-one groups to detect saturation. Period visitors retain provider semantics instead of summing daily unique visitors. Runtime validation rejects invalid/duplicate/out-of-window days, unsafe counts, inconsistent totals and query echoes. Non-atomic upstream snapshots may drift; a disagreement is unavailable, not silently accepted as complete. Metadata is projected out.

One twenty-second helper budget, twelve-second provider phase limits, caller cancellation, fixed origin, refused redirects and actual 256KiB UTF-8 response limits bound external work. Auth/read/whole-route budgets are ten/five/twenty-five seconds. The browser uses a twenty-second deadline, 32KiB response bound and strict owner receipt. Streams are released without waiting for uncooperative cancellation, including late headers. Private producer/auth/database errors never enter UI responses. Complete successes alone cache for sixty seconds, with isolated clones and at most fifty hashed credential/owner/team/repository/window keys. Offline transport counts show four requests cold and no additional requests on an immediate repeat; expiration or synthetic credential rotation causes a new four-request read. No concurrent server request coalescing or production cost/latency reduction is claimed.

The component uses owner-scoped query keys/lifetimes, no automatic retry and stable EN/CS formatters. Cold failures expose Retry; failed refreshes keep confirmed metrics with one alert. Disabled and public-preview contexts render no pending card or private request. Long figures can wrap instead of widening a grid. Thirteen mounted component cases cover recovery, unknown states, owner mismatch, retry admission and unmount cancellation. Four native EN/CS desktop/mobile project cases pass in 11.9 seconds: six widths each, light/dark, axe, no overflow/errors/private analytics requests. Native evidence is preview/workspace geometry; live metric/error layouts are mounted evidence.

The first deadline tests used fake global timers without replacing Node's native AbortSignal.timeout and timed out; controlled timeout signals corrected the harness. One retry test expected a disabled button although cold retry intentionally shows loading; its assertion now checks that state. Those diagnostic runs are not passing evidence. All 94 final targeted cases pass across five files in 2.90 seconds; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. React review confirms stable owner keys, captured cancellation, retained confirmed data, hoisted formatters and no eager guest request. All credentials/transports/identities are synthetic; no live Vercel, owner-data write, publication or migration runs. Primary endpoint contracts are linked in the checkpoint 81 review above. Final complete release, thirty-two migration rollouts and main merge remain unfinished.


## Finding 212 — future and malformed cached VAT evidence

## Finding 213 — bounded, identity-matched ARES and VIES routes

Six genuine failures on the unchanged routes reproduce null-body exceptions, accepted cached-user auth errors and successful replies for a different registration number. The shared two-route read-only handler now admits only strict single-field JSON within 1KiB, with a five-second whole-upload limit. Auth has an eight-second budget and rejects errors even when a cached user accompanies them; rate admission waits at most two seconds. The whole handler is twenty-five seconds. Responses are private/no-store, owner-stamped and projected through bounded schemas; an optional captured-owner header is enforced when supplied. The browser has not yet adopted mandatory captured receipts, so that lifecycle remains open. No service role or registry persistence is introduced.

The fixed ARES origin uses a ten-second phase and 256KiB actual UTF-8 bound; VIES uses fifteen seconds and 64KiB. Both refuse redirects, disable transport caching, pass cancellation, release non-success and partial streams, and observe/release headers arriving after a deadline. Auth/rate/provider exceptions produce fixed safe errors. ARES absence remains 404, while an unreadable/outage response is 502. VIES transport, malformed/mismatched identity and member-state outages remain status unavailable, never an invalid verdict. Valid/invalid verdicts require the exact echoed country and number plus a boolean result; withheld trader name/address remain supported. Existing outage-preservation and VAT/invoice arithmetic are unchanged.

The [primary ARES OpenAPI](https://ares.gov.cz/ekonomicke-subjekty-v-be/rest/v3/api-docs) supplies projected field types and bounds, including its full 2,000-character legal name. It is accepted without truncation. The [official VIES test WSDL](https://ec.europa.eu/taxation_customs/vies/checkVatTestService.wsdl) publishes synthetic 100/200/301 scenarios. Three calls to the Commission's separate [REST test service](https://ec.europa.eu/taxation_customs/vies/rest-api/check-vat-test-service) confirm matching country/number, boolean valid/invalid and the distinct actionSucceed/errorWrappers outage shape. Only CZ plus those synthetic test numbers were sent; no production/customer number or credential lookup runs. The main validation WSDL and Commission explanatory links remain above. These test-service results establish response-shape evidence, not a legal/customer validation or deployed integration receipt.

All 95 targeted route/registry/shared-body cases pass across four files in 419ms; TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. They cover actual multibyte bounds, malformed/extra input, exact identity, primary field limits, null trader fields, CSRF, unauthenticated/cached-user errors, rate refusal, stalled upload/auth/limiter/headers, caller disconnect and nonblocking cancellation. Four native Next-route admission cases pass in 4.1 seconds across desktop/mobile projects, exercising twenty-eight local HTTP requests for malformed JSON fields, oversized input, cross-origin refusal and invalid UTF-8 before provider admission. No UI is synthesized and no owner data or migration changes. Browser captured mutations, significant VAT syntax, the remaining source leads and final complete release remain unfinished.

Existing Czech formatting, stored-evidence invalidation, outage preservation and invoice math tests remain passing. Browser action/receipt lifecycle and final complete release remain open.


## Finding 215 — ARES draft races and client-form geometry

Five regressions against the preceding component reproduce late draft application, overwritten newer name text and accepted foreign receipts. The new-form lookup now captures the owner/lifetime and draft generation, reserves one request synchronously and cancels on registration input changes, close/reopen, unmount or owner disposal. It accepts only a strict matching owner/number receipt with a real returned timestamp, keeps typed billing fields and preserves name edits made after the lookup began. A lookup cannot race the Create action; closing a read cancels it without blocking navigation. No record persistence claim is made by this read-only repair.

The shared client reader bounds actual response bytes at 32KiB, uses one twenty-eight-second deadline and captured cancellation, refuses redirects, discards HTTP error bodies and releases late headers. Both ARES and VIES typed contracts are tested; only the new-form ARES caller adopts the reader here. Existing-client registry writes, create/delete confirmation, complete organization reads and draft-exit persistence remain open. No fallback timestamp or private producer text is accepted. Draft feedback stores a translation key, so changing language during the request uses the current dictionary. ## Finding 216 — real organization versions and a complete-read foundation

The unchanged 118-migration probe confirms ordinary organization writes do not maintain a version and an earlier-version predicate still accepts stale changes. ## Finding 218 — confirmed organization write foundation

The shared organization boundary now captures owner, source fields and database version before waiting. Updates and deletions filter by id, owner and the exact PostgreSQL timestamp. Each operation requires one complete matching returned row; updates also require an advanced version and unchanged fields outside the patch. A zero-row result reports a conflict. No callback may replace a newer cached source. Submitted patches reject extra/undefined fields and payloads above 64KiB. The SDK parses responses before validation; this is not a streamed response-byte bound.

Creation accepts a stable caller-supplied id and all intended/default fields. An explicit retry after a lost response can recover only the same owned id with the exact fields. A duplicate never becomes an upsert. One fifteen-second allowance covers the request and any duplicate recovery, with caller cancellation, no hidden retry and fixed safe errors. Cancellation does not promise to roll back a committed database request. The UI must retain that id across an uncertain response; the current client controls still need adoption.

All 54 final local organization read/write/version cases pass across three files in 8.35 seconds. Real SDK requests run under PostgreSQL RLS and cover lost-response creation recovery, changed duplicate refusal, microsecond versions, stale update/delete refusal, switched actors, exact filters, malformed/expanded receipts, unrelated-field preservation, deadlines, disposal and source-safe cache application. Final TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass, including captured-scope and undefined-patch checks. No production record or policy changes. Migration 119 supplies the version trigger; the prepared migration chain, client adoption, consolidated release and main merge remain unfinished.

## Finding 230 — avoid redundant confirmed-write collection reads

Three active React Query store regressions show one extra complete-list query after each confirmed create, update and deletion. These checks establish one avoided collection request per confirmed action, not a measured production latency or dollar saving. The mounted harness now uses the actual useEntityStore cache/notification path rather than a parallel React state array. Removal focus waits for that cache notification and abandons its intent if a changed row wins.

A native regression against the first optimization reproduces a saved row clearing React Query's old collection error, then losing the stale-data warning after opening the project tab. The shell now retains that owner-stamped read failure independently of row writes. Only a complete successful collection read clears it; aborted/disposed reads cannot change it. This adds no network query or private persistent storage. Confirmed editing remains available while the rest of the list is explicitly stale.

## Finding 231 — bounded Competition rendering and complete local search

Three preceding-source mounted regressions reproduce all 1,201 sample rows mounting, an unexplained filtered-empty area and research disappearing when its cached project is unavailable. Both real surfaces now mount at most fifty research rows, with one localized range and equal Previous/Next controls above the list. Paging, search, clear and project/category/review filters stay local to the complete owned snapshot. Search covers records beyond the first page; filter changes reset the page and a shorter refresh clamps it. Missing project details receive a named fallback group instead of discarding the research or offering an invalid project action. Only groups containing filtered research render, removing repeated empty cards.

The full destination and project tab share a centered 1,024px maximum. Short EN/CS guidance and narrower consistent filters keep the desktop controls compact; mobile keeps full targets. An empty result explains the state and offers Clear filters, which restores search focus. Page controls remain above the rows, so changing a long list does not strand the reader at its footer. Project-tab instances follow project identity, disposing the previous editor/action lifetime when the actual project changes; ordinary dirty navigation still uses the existing guard.

Project ordering indexes children and membership once while retaining canonical registry, child, client and researched-inactive order. Search builds normalized text only when a query is nonblank and reuses it across keystrokes. Review counts and project-specific sorting/filtering reuse unchanged inputs rather than repeating on every draft edit. The [offline CPU probe](competition-cpu.json) extracts the actual before/after source expressions, includes search-index construction and confirms identical project ids and all twenty query results. Five samples over 5,000 projects/10,000 synthetic research rows take 102.64–134.34ms before versus 1.84–2.63ms after for ordering, and 937.49–970.64ms versus 74.54–75.84ms for twenty searches. These are local expression measurements, not production latency, database traffic or provider spend.

All 82 final mounted/domain/write cases pass across three files in 4.35 seconds. TypeScript and targeted ESLint pass. Twenty-six EN/CS desktop/mobile native cases pass in 1.2 minutes. A complete 1,201-record reply mounts fifty rows; pagination and a last-record/missing-project search add no collection request. Both real surfaces page locally, clear/filter recovery preserves data, and the centered maximum, eight widths, axe and prior version/draft/refresh/focus checks pass. The synthetic active-cache tests also cover shorter-refresh clamping and every paging boundary. Production migration rollout, other source leads, consolidated release, main merge and deployed verification remain open.
